# Life Reset: 66 Day Habit (App Store ID 6478942469) — Full Review Analysis

**App:** Life Reset: 66 Day Habit — *Gamified Habit Tracker* (shipped as **Rise: Life Reset in 66 Days** until mid-2025; early reviewers call it "Rise")
**Developer:** Savvy Tech Limited (bundle `build.designand.rise`)
**Corpus:** 1,232 written App Store reviews, 75 storefronts, 16 April 2024 – 7 September 2026
**Extracted:** 2026-09-08T11:53:50Z · **Analysis date:** 12 September 2026
**Source:** `app store review/49. Life Reset - 66 Day Habit - Gamified Habit Tracker/`

---

## How to read this

Every number is a count of **written reviews in this corpus**, never of users, downloads or revenue. Themes are **non-exclusive**: one review can carry several, so theme percentages sum to more than 100%. The denominator is always stated. Signal labels follow the thresholds in `GENERALIZED_STORE_REVIEW_ANALYSIS_PROMPT.md` (§ Signal thresholds) and are applied to the global corpus and to each eligible storefront separately.

Review IDs in backticks are exact `review_id` values from `reviews.jsonl`. §10.7 resolves every one of the 1,232 reviews to its themes; §10.8 resolves every theme to its reviews. Any number in this document can be walked back to the exact reviews that produced it, and any review can be walked forward to the findings it supports.

### ⚠️ Eight warnings before any number here is used

1. **The written corpus is not the rating corpus, and here the gap is enormous.** The App Store listing shows **4.7★ from ~12,000 ratings** (accessed 12 September 2026); the 1,232 people who also wrote text average **2.77★**. That is a ~1.9-star divergence — far larger than is typical — and §3.1 explains why: a quarter of all written reviews come from people who never got into the product at all. **Do not read 2.77★ as the app's standing.** Read it as the standing of the *onboarding funnel*, which is what most writers are reviewing.
2. **26.87% of this corpus (331 reviews, mean 1.23★) is written by people who never used the app.** They completed the questionnaire, hit the paywall, and left. They carry a paywall theme and *no* theme describing the product's behaviour, features, reliability or content. They are counted in every global denominator — they are real reviews — but they say almost nothing about whether the product works.
3. **The corpus is young and back-loaded.** 73.9% of it (910 reviews) was written in 2025 alone. There is no long history to compare against; the "pre-scale" era is 59 reviews. Trend claims rest on 2025–2026 movement, not on years of it.
4. **No review states a price in a verifiable way.** Reviewers quote $1.99/mo, $12.99, $13, $17.99, $18, $20, $23, $24.99, $25, $28.99, $30, $34.99, $39.99, $40, $43, $49.99, $50, $59.99, $60, $65, $70, $79.99, $80, $87, £2, £13, £20, £24.99, £25, £29.99, £50, €12, €14, €15, €20, €30, €50, €60, €70, €79.99, €80, zł45, zł60, AU$17.98, AU$23, AU$39, AU$80, CA$24.99, CA$79.99 and ¥3,000–¥4,500 across 29 months and many currencies. Prices are reported as **dated reviewer claims**, never as a price history. The store listing's ten distinct in-app purchase price points (§2.3) confirm the range is genuinely wide.
5. **Payer identification is self-reported.** 106 reviews (8.60%) contain first-person evidence of paying. That is a **floor on paid reviewers, not a conversion rate**, and nothing in this corpus supports a conversion estimate.
6. **Payer reviews are not a satisfaction sample.** Payers here average **1.92★** — well below the corpus — because people who paid and were satisfied largely wrote short praise without mentioning payment, while people who paid and were *not* satisfied said so explicitly. §6 treats the payer segment as an inventory of post-purchase failure modes, not as a measure of paid satisfaction.
7. **Absence of a theme is not absence of the problem.** 28 reviews (2.27%) contain no assessable product content at all ("Good", "H", "7", a single emoji). They are counted, never mined.
8. **Sentiment is inferred from text, not from stars.** 27 reviews (2.19%) contradict their own star rating outright — 5★ reviews that are complaints, 1★ reviews that are praise. They are flagged (`J09`) and disclosed in §5.7 rather than dropped or silently re-scored.

---

# EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

**Life Reset is a well-liked product with a broken front door.** Among reviewers who actually got inside, the verdict is strong and specific: 167 reviews (13.56%, mean **4.78★**) report real behaviour change, 60 (4.87%, mean 4.55★) praise the level-up mechanic by name, and 38 (3.08%, mean **4.87★**) say the Solo Leveling framing is exactly what they wanted. But **43.67% of the entire corpus (538 reviews, mean 1.49★) attacks how the app is sold** — and those 538 reviews supply **74.6% of every 1–2★ review in the corpus**. The product is not the problem. The funnel is.

**1. One design decision explains most of this corpus: a 10–20 minute questionnaire in front of a hard paywall.** `B01` (hard paywall, no free use) is **256 reviews, 20.78%, mean 1.31★** — the single largest theme of any kind. `B02` (onboarding completed before the price is disclosed) is **156 reviews, 12.66%, mean 1.15★** — the lowest mean of any theme with more than 20 reviews. Together they account for **48.6% of all 1–2★ reviews**. The grievance is remarkably consistent across 75 storefronts and 20-plus languages, and it is almost always the same sentence: *I spent 10–20 minutes answering personal questions, signed a pledge, and only then learned it costs money.* 37 reviews (3.00%) add that the ad or listing said "free". 20 (1.62%) frame it as a data-collection problem. 35 (2.84%) name the funnel itself — the pledges, the "lock in" repetition, the countdown discount — as manipulation.

**2. The free-trial question is the highest-leverage open decision, and the corpus has now tested both answers.** 90 reviews (7.31%, mean 1.32★) explicitly ask for a trial. A trial **did not exist** for most of the corpus's life. From **24 January 2026** it appears: 11 reviews (`B18`) describe being charged immediately on signing up for a stated free trial, and trial-use mentions (`C06`) go from 0.20% of the pre-24-Jan-2026 corpus to 2.85% after. The result is the most important negative finding in this report: **introducing the trial did not reduce paywall anger — it converted it into billing anger.** `B01` actually *rose* from 17.24% to **34.96%** across that boundary, and `B09` ("advertised as free") from 2.13% to 6.50%. A trial that requires a card, auto-charges, and is hard to exit has bought the developer the reputational cost of a trial without the trust benefit.

**3. For people who do pay, the complaint is not price — it is rigidity.** 80 reviews (6.49%) say they cannot add their own tasks; 70 (5.68%) say the generated programme cannot be edited or removed. Among confirmed payers these run **9.43%** and **15.09%**. The specific failures recur verbatim: running is the only cardio (20 reviews, plus 10 from people with injury, disability, asthma or nerve damage who cannot do it — 23 distinct reviews); the wake-up window excludes night-shift workers (31 reviews, 23 of whom identify as shift workers); the weekly ramp reaches 90-minute lifts, 4-mile runs and 2 gallons of water for beginners (20 reviews); the day closes at 9pm regardless of when the user's day ends (10 reviews). These are the reviews from people who wanted to stay. They are also the ones most likely to sit at 3–4★, which is where `E01` and `E02` peak — **17.65% and 14.41% of the 3★ band**.

**4. Paying customers report problems at nearly nine times the rate of everyone else, and support does not catch them.** **91.5% of confirmed payers (97 of 106)** carry at least one complaint theme. Reliability themes appear in **41.5% of payer reviews versus 13.4% of non-payers**; `D04` (progress reset or lost) is 14.15% of payers versus 2.92% globally, `D05` (login failure) 11.32% versus 1.30%, `B08` (overcharged) 8.49% versus 0.73%. Meanwhile 21 reviews (1.70%, mean 1.43★) say support is unreachable — a dead button, a 404, a bouncing address, or silence — and **10 of those 21 are payers**. 36 reviews request refunds; **21 report being refused or ignored**, and 13 invoke a "66-day money-back guarantee" that several say did not materialise. This is the corpus's clearest reputational risk: it is the paying customers who are being left without recourse.

**5. Reliability is episodic, not chronic — and the episodes are datable.** The whole Reliability family is 15.83% of the corpus, modest for a young app. But it clusters into five identifiable windows: a launch-crash cluster in October 2024 (`D01` at 37.5% of that window), a **22 May 2025 update that stopped the app opening at all** (`D01`+`D13` at 37.8% of the following fortnight, from users 20–39 days into a 66-day programme), a mid-June 2025 logout wave (`D05`+`D04` at 20.5% of that window, mean 1.92★), an Aug–Sep 2025 signup-blocked cluster where the name field could not be scrolled past (15 reviews), and a June 2026 crash-on-task-completion wave (32.1% of that month). `D01` has fallen to **zero in the most recent era** — the crash problem was solved. `D18` (unspecified bugs) has **risen to 7.9%**, its highest ever, alongside a new `D17` (hero/character-mode bugs) that did not exist before 2026.

**6. The AI objection is real, durable, and aimed at craft rather than function.** 53 reviews (4.30%, mean 1.85★) criticise AI-generated art, copy or book summaries. It is the only product-criticism theme that **rises across every era** (0% → 4.4% → 4.0% → 5.5%), and it is disproportionately Australian (10.34%) and American (4.66%). Reviewers do not object to AI planning — 4 reviews praise it — they object to paying a premium price for assets they read as uncommissioned. Several say explicitly that they would recommend the app if the art were commissioned.

**7. The Solo Leveling positioning is the growth engine and the expectation trap.** 38 reviews (3.08%, mean 4.87★) name the anime as the reason they love it; 15 reviews (1.22%, mean 1.40★) name it as the reason they feel misled — they expected an avatar, quests and penalties and found a checklist. 38 reviews (3.08%, mean 1.68★) reduce the product to "just a checklist". 15 more (1.22%) name Instagram or TikTok as where they found it. The marketing is working; it is writing a cheque the product only partly cashes. The developer appears to know this: a character/hero mode shipped in late 2025 and immediately generated its own bug theme.

**8. There is a genuine, ignored, well-defined segment being turned away: shift workers.** 31 reviews (2.52%, mean **3.42★** — notably higher than the corpus) describe being unable to set a wake time outside a roughly 5am–10am window. 23 explicitly identify as night, graveyard or FIFO workers. Their mean rating is high because they *want the product*; several say so in the same breath as uninstalling. This is the cheapest high-value fix in the report.

**The strategic read:** Life Reset has built something a devoted minority calls life-changing, wrapped in an acquisition funnel that generates more one-star reviews than the product generates five-star ones. Fixing the product is not the priority — 40.18% of the corpus is already 4–5★, and the praise is unusually specific. The priorities are, in order: **disclose the price before the questionnaire**, **make the trial a real trial**, **let people edit their own programme**, and **answer paying customers when they write in**. Every one of those four is supported by a high-priority signal, and three of them are cheap.

# PART 1 — SCOPE AND METHOD

## 1.1 Files used

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `app store review/49. .../reviews.jsonl` | Primary corpus — 1,232 records, one JSON object per line |
| `app store review/49. .../by_country/*.jsonl` | 75 per-storefront files, 1,232 records total |
| `app store review/49. .../manifest.json` | App metadata, per-country counts, rating distribution |
| `app store review/49. .../_state.json` | Per-storefront collection cursors and completion flags |

Working artifacts (gitignored, `temp/`): `49-dump.py`, `49-reconcile.py`, `49-themes.py`, `49-idx-classification.py`, `49-build-classification.py`, `49-review-classification.py`, `49-validate.py`, `49-common.py`, `49-aggregate.py`, `49-trends.py`, `49-extra.py`, `49-tables.py`, `49-build-report.py`, `49-verify-report.py`.

## 1.2 Schema

Every record carries exactly these 13 fields, with **no missing or empty values anywhere in the 1,232**:

`review_id`, `app_id`, `app_name`, `country`, `country_name`, `rating`, `title`, `body`, `author`, `date`, `vote_count`, `vote_sum`, `is_edited`.

Fields used in this analysis: `review_id` (identity), `country`/`country_name` (market), `rating` (1–5), `title` + `body` (classification), `date` (trend), `is_edited` and `vote_count`/`vote_sum` (integrity checks). `author` was read during classification but is not reported: **no reviewer is named in this document**. `app_id` is constant (`6478942469`) and `app_name` is constant (`Life Reset: 66 Day Habit`) across all records, including reviews written when the app was still called Rise.

HTML entities in `title`/`body` (`&#39;`, `&amp;`, `&quot;`, and in some records the double-escaped `&amp;#39;`) were unescaped for reading and classification; raw values in `reviews.jsonl` are unchanged.

## 1.3 Coverage and reconciliation

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Records in `reviews.jsonl` | **1,232** |
| Unique `review_id` values | **1,232** (zero duplicates) |
| Records across the 75 `by_country/*.jsonl` files | **1,232** |
| Country-file IDs absent from main file | **0** |
| Main-file IDs absent from country files | **0** |
| Per-country counts, country files vs main file | **identical for all 75** |
| `manifest.json` `total_reviews` vs actual | 1,232 vs **1,232** ✓ |
| `manifest.json` `storefronts_with_reviews` vs observed | 75 vs **75** ✓ |
| `manifest.json` `rating_distribution` vs actual | 5★ 359 / 4★ 136 / 3★ 111 / 2★ 119 / 1★ 507 — **exact match** ✓ |
| `manifest.json` `mean_rating` vs actual | 2.774 vs **2.7735** ✓ (rounding only) |
| `manifest.json` `reviews_per_country` vs actual | **exact match for all 75** ✓ |
| `_state.json` | all 140 listed storefronts are `complete: true`; 65 returned zero written reviews and 75 returned at least one ✓ |
| `_state.json` `collected` vs per-country actuals | **matches for every storefront** ✓ |
| `_state.json` `collected` vs `total` | **equal for every storefront** — no partial collection ✓ |
| **Reviews read individually** | **1,232 (100%)** |
| **Reviews classified** | **1,232 (100%)** — zero unassigned, zero unknown IDs, zero empty theme lists |

**No deduplication was performed.** One group of exact text duplicates exists and is flagged rather than merged: `13357929714` (CA), `14389884342` (TH) and `14445102588` (TH), all titled "Good" with body "Good". These are three different storefronts and three different reviewers writing the same trivial word; they are not a technical duplicate and each remains a separate record in every denominator. The effect on any percentage is under 0.25pp.

**Integrity checks run and passed** (`49-validate.py`, 11 assertions): no duplicate source IDs; the classification map covers exactly the source ID set; no unknown IDs; no unassigned records; no duplicated theme inside a single review; no undefined theme codes; no orphan theme definitions; the working-index map is contiguous 1..1232; the index-keyed and ID-keyed maps agree for all 1,232; every theme resolves to a defined family.

## 1.4 Date range and shape

First review `11164609337` (Russia, 4★, 16 April 2024). Last review `14519880651` (France, 4★, 7 September 2026). Span: **2 years, 4.7 months**.

| Year | n | % of corpus | Mean ★ | 5★ | 4★ | 3★ | 2★ | 1★ | 1–2★ share | US |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2024 (from 16 Apr) | 52 | 4.2% | 2.58 | 10 | 8 | 5 | 8 | 21 | 55.8% | 7 |
| 2025 | 910 | 73.9% | 2.76 | 249 | 109 | 91 | 94 | 367 | 50.7% | 503 |
| 2026 (to 7 Sep) | 270 | 21.9% | 2.87 | 100 | 19 | 15 | 17 | 119 | 50.4% | 69 |

Body length: median **152 characters**, mean 221, minimum 1 (`11825234292`, a single "🌞"), maximum 3,459 (`13073292592`, US, 5★ — a six-section essay). 98 reviews have a body of 20 characters or fewer.

**The corpus is dominated by a single 2025 ramp.** Monthly volume goes 1–13 reviews per month through 2024, then steps up in February 2025 and peaks at **146 in August 2025**. This coincides with the Instagram/TikTok campaign that 15 reviews name directly. 2026 volume is roughly a third of the 2025 rate.

**The 1–2★ share is remarkably stable at ~50% across all three years** (55.8% / 50.7% / 50.4%) while the mean drifts slightly upward. The negative half of the corpus is not shrinking; the positive half is getting more positive (5★ share rises from 19.2% of 2024 to 37.0% of 2026).

Full monthly detail is in §8.2.

## 1.5 Processing method

1. **Parse and profile** (`49-dump.py`): load all 1,232 records, unescape HTML entities, sort by (`date`, `review_id`), assign a stable working index 1..1232, emit one working file plus 21 sixty-review chunks.
2. **Reconcile** (`49-reconcile.py`): cross-check the main file against 75 country files, `manifest.json` and `_state.json`. Results in §1.3.
3. **Read every review individually**, in its original language, in date order, in the 21 chunks. Reviews appear in Japanese, Korean, Polish, German, Spanish, Portuguese, French, Italian, Dutch, Turkish, Ukrainian, Czech, Hungarian, Swedish, Danish, Indonesian, Vietnamese, Thai, Chinese and Arabic-script text as well as English; each was read in the original, not in translation, and classified on what it says rather than on keywords.
4. **Build the taxonomy from the corpus** (`49-themes.py`): 111 themes in 10 families, derived from what reviewers actually said rather than from a prior list. Boundary rules are recorded with each definition (§10.1) — in particular the deliberate separations of `B01` (a price exists) from `B02` (the price was disclosed too late), of `E01` (cannot add) from `E02` (cannot edit), of `F02` (AI criticised) from `F03` (AI valued), of `E14` (persona misfit) from `F09` (ideological objection), and of `C03` (refund refused) from `I01` (support unreachable).
5. **Classify by hand, keyed by working index** (`49-idx-classification.py`): every judgement made while reading, recorded against the index rather than the 11-digit review ID to remove transcription risk. Assignments are non-exclusive; the mean is **2.49 themes per review** (3,073 assignments over 1,232 reviews).
6. **Resolve and validate** (`49-build-classification.py` → `49-review-classification.py`, then `49-validate.py`): deterministically map index → `review_id` from `reviews.jsonl`, emit the auditable `review_id → themes` map, then run the 11 integrity assertions in §1.3. The report is not written unless all 11 pass.
7. **Aggregate** (`49-aggregate.py`, `49-trends.py`, `49-extra.py`): counts, rating bands, payer segment, storefronts, market groups, eras, months, named incident windows, per-country theme profiles.
8. **Generate tables** (`49-tables.py`) and **assemble** (`49-build-report.py`), which refuses to write if any placeholder is unfilled or any of 22 count checks disagrees with the recomputed data.
9. **Verify after writing** (`49-verify-report.py`): every review ID cited anywhere in this document exists in `reviews.jsonl`; every §10.7 index row matches both the map and the source record; every §10.8 ID list matches the map exactly.

**No sampling, no keyword filtering, no stopping at a context limit.** Every one of the 1,232 reviews was read and classified.

## 1.6 Limitations and known biases

- **Selection bias is the dominant limitation and it is unusually severe here.** The store shows 4.7★ from ~12,000 ratings; this corpus of the ~10% who also wrote text averages 2.77★. The written corpus over-represents people who bounced off the paywall, because bouncing off a paywall is a complete, articulable experience that takes 20 minutes to acquire and 30 seconds to write up. Nothing in this report should be read as the app's overall standing.
- **The "never used it" cohort distorts every product-quality percentage.** 331 reviews (26.87%) describe only the funnel. When measuring anything about the product itself, the honest denominator is closer to 901. §4.1 restates the main product findings on that basis.
- **Payer self-identification is a floor, not a rate.** 106 reviews (8.60%) say they paid. Many more certainly did without saying so — every review describing day 40 of a programme is from a payer — so the payer segment is skewed toward people whose payment became salient because something went wrong.
- **Recency is thin in absolute terms.** The last 12 months hold 512 reviews (41.6%), which is healthy, but the *most recent* era (E4, from 1 December 2025) is 290 reviews. Themes newer than that — `D17` hero-mode bugs (5 reviews), `B18` trial billing (11) — are genuine but small.
- **Only five storefronts clear the 50-review bar** (US, CA, DE, GB, AU — 909 reviews, 73.78%). Seventy storefronts do not, including Japan (31), Poland (30) and South Korea (22), which show distinctive patterns that this report labels as limited evidence rather than findings.
- **The US is 47.00% of the corpus.** Global percentages are heavily US percentages; §7.10 quantifies the difference.
- **Prices, plan names and feature availability changed during the window** — the app was renamed, a character mode was added, a trial appeared in January 2026. A theme's count aggregates across product versions unless §8 separates them.
- **Rating is not a feature preference.** A 5★ review that asks for a feature is a request, not an endorsement of its absence; a 1★ review that praises the design is disclosed, not re-scored. 27 reviews (`J09`) contradict their own stars outright.
- **Classification error risk.** Borderline calls concentrate in three places: `B01` vs `B02` where a review complains about both the existence and the timing of the price (both assigned where both are stated); `E01` vs `E02` where "can't customise" is ambiguous between adding and editing (assigned by which the reviewer describes, both where both); and `D18` vs a specific reliability theme where "buggy" carries no symptom (`D18` only when no symptom is named). Every theme with fewer than 10 reviews was re-read end to end before publication, as were all 106 payer reviews, all 40 scam-accusation reviews and all 27 star/text contradictions.
- **This corpus cannot establish causation.** It can show that `B01` rose after the trial appeared; it cannot show the trial caused it. Alternative explanations — a concurrent ad campaign, a price change, seasonality — are not observable here and are flagged wherever a trend is claimed.

# PART 2 — PRODUCT AND MONETIZATION MODEL

## 2.1 Feature inventory derived from reviews

Everything below is what **reviewers describe experiencing**. Nothing is taken from marketing copy. Counts are reviews mentioning the feature in any direction.

| Capability | What reviewers describe | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **66-day structured programme** | A fixed-length course of daily tasks, auto-generated from an onboarding questionnaire, with difficulty escalating each week. The organising idea of the product. | `13073292592`, `12181485638`, `13200`-era reviews passim; ramp complaints `13329009131`, `12999503079` |
| **Daily task checklist** | 5–8 preset tasks per day — wake early, drink water, run, workout, read, meditate, journal, no social media, cold shower, pray, no-fap — marked done / partially done / skipped by swipe. | `12917102928`, `12576463690`, `13110133783` |
| **XP, stats and levelling** | An "overall" rating plus category stats (Strength, Wisdom, Focus, Discipline) that rise as tasks complete. The single most-praised mechanic. | `A02` — 60 reviews; `12900289560`, `13654164676`, `12315868340` |
| **Hard mode** | Optional strict mode: miss a day or skip a task and the programme resets to day 1, with penalty tasks. | `A17` — 13 reviews; `13072368493`, `12936813410`, `13037385252` |
| **Character / hero mode** | A visual avatar with equipment, loot and battles. Appears in reviews only from **late 2025**; generates its own bug theme immediately. | `13592233963`, `13596312321`, `14195130483`, `13780831362` |
| **Achievements and motivation cards** | Collectible achievement art and motivational cards. | `12366219056`, `12955163393`, `13189323301` |
| **Streaks and day counter** | Day N / 66 plus a separate day-streak and XP multiplier. A frequent source of bugs. | `D10` — 11 reviews; `14116238467`, `13406494249` |
| **Book summaries** | A library of condensed non-fiction. Widely liked; widely reported as English-only. | `G04` — 23 reviews, mean 4.09★; `12888691060`, `13406026400` |
| **Guided meditation** | In-app audio meditation. Several reports of it failing or sounding synthetic. | `A11`, `D14`; `12979102442`, `14508328179`, `13229989580` |
| **App / screen blocker** | Blocks named apps to prevent doom-scrolling. Consistently praised where mentioned. | `12917102928`, `12937610877`, `12900289560` |
| **Pomodoro / time blocks** | A focus timer. | `12764415874`, `12900289560` |
| **Workout tracker** | Exercise logging with a fixed exercise list (~15 entries), no custom entries. | `12883609029`, `12865447391`, `13324975532` |
| **Calorie tracker** | Present in 2026 reviews only; buggy settings persistence reported. | `13838798854`, `14181754070`, `13900765197` |
| **Journal** | Added during 2025 per one reviewer's before/after account. | `12917164876`, `13046112649` |
| **Home-screen widget** | Frequently reported blank, stuck on day 1, or showing wrong icons. | `D08` — 8 reviews; `12471607720`, `12604750095`, `14519880651` |
| **Daily recap / reflection** | An end-of-day summary that fires on a fixed schedule (commonly reported as 9pm) and closes scoring for the day. | `E11` — 10 reviews; `13013314943`, `13018958577`, `13072200651` |
| **Discord community** | An official server; the developer is described as active in it. Strongly positive where mentioned. | `A09` — 17 reviews, mean 4.65★; `13029589554`, `13339495032` |
| **Referral codes** | At least one reviewer posts a referral code, implying a referral programme. | `13118822768` |
| **Multi-platform** | iPhone, iPad and Mac builds exist; iPad and Mac layouts are repeatedly reported broken. | `D12` — 15 reviews; `12945514746`, `12963409255`, `12969618294` |
| **Localisation** | German added around May 2025 (`12634875883` notes it as new). Book summaries and meditations remain English-only in 2026. | `G01`/`G02` — 24 distinct reviews |
| **Under-18 / student free programme** | Three reviewers describe getting free access as a student or under-18. Not visible in the store listing. | `J02` — `12921714751`, `12944427920`, `12992530011` |
| **66-day money-back guarantee** | Marketed; 13 reviews reference it. Several report it requires 66 logged days at ≥50% completion, which they say was not clear. | `C05`; `13045702582`, `13346302196`, `14203716713` |

## 2.2 Free / paid classification

| Tier | What the corpus establishes |
|---|---|
| **Free** | The onboarding questionnaire, the personalised "analysis" result, and the paywall itself. **Nothing else.** 256 reviews state this directly. |
| **Paid — everything** | Daily quests, XP and levelling, streaks, hard mode, the book library, meditation, the app blocker, the workout and calorie trackers, widgets, and customisation. No reviewer describes a usable free tier at any point in the 29-month window. |
| **Trial — absent, then present** | No trial for most of the window (90 reviews ask for one). From **24 January 2026** a trial exists, and 11 reviews describe being charged at signup or at trial end without effective warning (`B18`). |
| **Plan structure** | Monthly and annual. 15 reviews (`B06`) object to the structure specifically: a 66-day programme sold as an annual subscription, monthly priced so that two months exceed the year, or annual-only at checkout. |
| **Refunds** | An App Store refund is what most reviewers attempt. 21 of 36 refund requests report refusal or silence. |
| **Unclear** | Whether the under-18/student free programme is an official tier or a case-by-case concession. Three reviews reference it; the store listing does not. |

## 2.3 External sources

One external source was consulted, and everything drawn from it is confined to this section.

**Apple App Store listing for Life Reset: 66 Day Habit App (`id6478942469`), accessed 12 September 2026.** It records: developer **Savvy Tech Limited**; subtitle *Gamified Habit Tracker*; category **Health & Fitness**; age rating **9+**; price model **Free with In-App Purchases**; current version **6.5.3**; and an average rating of **4.7★ from approximately 12,000 ratings**.

The listing enumerates **ten distinct in-app purchase price points**: `$12.99`, `$17.99`, `$28.99`, `$34.99`, `$39.99`, `$49.99`, `$59.99` under the name *Life Reset (Full Access)*, plus `$12.99`, `$28.99` and `$49.99` under the legacy name *Rise: Life reset in 66 days*.

Three things follow, and only these three:

1. **The rename is confirmed.** Both product names are live in the same purchase list, which corroborates the reviews from 2024–mid-2025 that call the app "Rise".
2. **The wide price range reviewers report is real, not confusion.** Ten price points in one listing is consistent with reviewers quoting figures from $12.99 to $80 and disagreeing with each other. This supports treating every quoted price as a dated reviewer claim (§1.6) rather than reconciling them.
3. **The 4.7★ / 2.77★ divergence is the single most important context in this report.** The store average and this corpus measure different populations. Warning 1 in the front matter exists because of this line.

The listing also states that core features require subscription and that onboarding is free — which matches the corpus exactly and is the one point where external and review-derived evidence fully agree.

No other external source was used. No claim anywhere else in this document depends on external data.

---

# PART 3 — GLOBAL FINDINGS

## 3.1 The shape of the corpus in one table

| Family | Themes | Reviews carrying ≥1 | % of 1,232 | Mean★ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| A — Core praise | 19 | 436 | 35.39% | 4.57 |
| B — Monetization friction | 19 | 538 | 43.67% | 1.49 |
| C — Purchase evidence | 6 | 138 | 11.20% | 1.99 |
| D — Reliability | 18 | 195 | 15.83% | 2.38 |
| E — Rigidity and customisation | 15 | 225 | 18.26% | 3.06 |
| F — Product-value criticism | 11 | 204 | 16.56% | 1.96 |
| G — Content and localisation | 5 | 63 | 5.11% | 3.59 |
| H — Feature requests | 8 | 56 | 4.55% | 3.64 |
| I — Support | 1 | 21 | 1.70% | 1.43 |
| J — Segments and meta | 9 | 118 | 9.58% | 3.31 |

Families are non-exclusive. Three structural facts follow from this table and they organise the rest of the report:

- **Monetization friction (538, 43.67%, mean 1.49★) is larger than core praise (436, 35.39%, mean 4.57★)** — and the two barely overlap. Only **35 reviews** carry both. The corpus is not a population with mixed feelings; it is two largely separate populations, one reviewing the funnel and one reviewing the product.
- **The negative half of the corpus is monetization-driven, not quality-driven.** Of the 626 reviews rated 1–2★, **467 (74.6%) carry a monetization theme**, while only 117 (18.7%) carry a reliability theme and 78 (12.5%) a rigidity theme. `B01` or `B02` alone account for **304 of the 626 (48.6%)**.
- **83 reviews (6.74%) carry no theme from A, B, D or E at all** — they are pure content, localisation, feature-request or meta reviews.

## 3.2 The "never used it" cohort — 331 reviews, 26.87%, mean 1.23★ · **high-priority**

**Definition:** a review carrying `B01`, `B02`, `B03` or `B09` and **no** theme from Core praise (A), Reliability (D) or Rigidity (E), and not a confirmed payer. These reviewers describe the funnel and nothing else, because they saw nothing else.

| Era | n in cohort | Era total | Cohort share of era |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| E1 pre-scale | 16 | 59 | 27.1% |
| E2 scale-up | 106 | 384 | 27.6% |
| E3 mid | 113 | 499 | 22.6% |
| E4 recent | 96 | 290 | 33.1% |

This cohort is **more than a quarter of everything written about this app**, it has not shrunk in 29 months, and in the most recent era it is at its largest. It is the single largest addressable block in the corpus and the cheapest to fix, because its complaint is not about the product.

Representative IDs: `11492314139`, `12324938124`, `12512390437`, `12611279186`, `12791093607`, `12938656842`, `13093074164`, `13288536663`, `13879554817`, `14101637932`, `14252648113`, `14474172527`.

## 3.3 Positive themes in detail

### 3.3.1 Behaviour change actually achieved — 167 reviews (13.56%, **high-priority**, mean 4.78★)

The largest praise theme and the second-largest theme of any kind. These are not "nice app" reviews: they describe a changed routine in the reviewer's own life. 139 of the 167 are 5★; only 4 are 1–3★. The claims are concrete and frequently physical — 30 pounds lost (`13010120367`), 8 pounds in 20 days (`12444052130`), quitting smoking (`13730150658`), quitting drinking (`13595919499`), returning to fitness after a second child (`12888691060`), resetting after retirement at 60-plus (`13838798854`), and functioning through severe depression (`13143280221`).

The theme **grows sharply**: 6.8% of E1, 6.8% of E2, **16.8% of E3, 18.3% of E4** (+11.5pp). That is the strongest positive trend in the corpus and it is the best single piece of evidence that the product improved during 2025.

IDs: `12181485638`, `12299418454`, `12315868340`, `12444052130`, `12673334430`, `12881029668`, `13010120367`, `13073292592`, `13143280221`, `13595919499`, `13730150658`, `13786079359`, `13838798854`, `14025544799`, `14118046964`, `14515700777`.

### 3.3.2 Gamification, Solo Leveling and the RPG frame — 60 + 38 reviews (4.87% + 3.08%)

`A02` (gamification loved, mean 4.55★) and `A03` (Solo Leveling / anime fit, mean **4.87★** — the highest mean of any theme above 30 reviews) are the product's differentiator. Reviewers describe the XP and stat system as the thing that distinguishes it from every other tracker they have used: *"it's like real time character upgrading"* (`12315868340`), *"levele wbija się w prawdziwym życiu"* — you gain levels in real life (`13278191408`, Polish), *"인생을 게임처럼 퀘스트 하나하나 클리어"* — clearing life like game quests (`12921470008`, Korean).

Both rise steadily across eras (`A02` 0% → 2.6% → 6.2% → 6.6%; `A03` 0% → 2.1% → 3.4% → 4.5%). 15 reviews (`H01`, mean 3.40★) want it pushed much further — skill trees, evolving avatars, weekly bosses, real penalties — and two of the corpus's most detailed reviews are essays on exactly this (`12449061267` in English, `13197705864` in Polish, the same argument written 6 months apart in two languages).

IDs (`A02`): `12315868340`, `12492042985`, `12900289560`, `12921470008`, `13029589554`, `13278191408`, `13654164676`, `13721297555`, `13743360224`, `14025544799`, `14497623684`. IDs (`A03`): `12634140436`, `12690161614`, `13068089792`, `13069261351`, `13441364129`, `13675335199`, `13713859618`, `13441364129` → see §10.8 for the complete list.

### 3.3.3 Accountability, structure and motivation — 39 + 40 reviews (3.17% + 3.25%, means 4.69★ / 4.60★)

`A05` and `A07` describe the mechanism reviewers say works: the app removes the daily decision. *"It helps to take the thinking out of what I need to do, and lets me focus on the doing portion"* (`13168157619`). *"I don't know what to do with myself if it doesn't tell me to workout"* (`12810086423`). This is a specific and defensible product claim, and it is why the rigidity complaints in §3.4.3 are so consequential: the same mechanism that helps beginners is what traps people whose lives do not fit the template.

### 3.3.4 The bundled tools and the book library — 22 + 23 reviews

`A11` (in-app tools, mean 4.00★) and `G04` (book summaries, mean 4.09★) are under-appreciated assets. The screen blocker in particular draws unprompted praise — *"the hard block screen blocker feature is a godsend as the typical screen time limit apple has as the base doesn't cut it"* (`12937610877`). The book summaries are liked well enough that the top request about them is for **more of them, in more languages, with audio** (`12567724340`, `12888691060`, `13406026400`, `14269329731`), not for their removal — even from reviewers who elsewhere criticise AI content.

### 3.3.5 Community and developer responsiveness — 17 + 19 reviews (means 4.65★ / 4.74★)

`A09` (Discord) and `A12` (developer listens, ships updates) are small but strategically important, and both **rise across eras** (`A12` 0% → 1.0% → 1.4% → 2.8%). Several reviewers say explicitly that the community and the developer's visible responsiveness are why they stayed after an initial doubt (`13029589554`, `13203380781`, `13339495032`, `13894859500`). One review (`12917164876`, Indonesia) is a complete before/after account: subscribed, abandoned the app within a week over rigidity, wrote to the developer, returned months later and found the customisation had been built — *"THIS APP HAS BEEN IMPROVED A LOT."*

That review is the corpus's proof that the rigidity problem is solvable and that the developer does act on feedback. `E15` (8 reviews) records the others who noticed.

### 3.3.6 Worth the money — 39 reviews (3.17%, **very strong**, mean 4.74★)

A counterweight worth stating plainly: 39 reviewers argue the price is fair, and several argue it directly against the one-star reviewers. *"All the bad reviews are just ridiculous. A bunch of other grown adults complaining about the price"* (`12927470549`). *"Yeah, You Have to Pay. Yeah, it's Worth it"* (`13894859500`). The defence is stable across eras (5.1% / 3.1% / 3.0% / 3.1%), which suggests a durable segment for whom the current price is simply not the issue.

## 3.4 Negative themes in detail

### 3.4.1 The paywall sequence — `B01` 256 (20.78%, mean 1.31★) and `B02` 156 (12.66%, mean 1.15★) · **high-priority**

The defining problem of this corpus. `B01` is the largest theme of any kind; `B02` has the **lowest mean rating of any theme above 20 reviews in the entire taxonomy**.

The two are distinct and the distinction matters for the fix. `B01` says *there is no free version*. `B02` says *I was not told until after I had invested 10–20 minutes*. 329 reviews carry one or both (26.7%). A substantial group carries **only** `B02` — they are not objecting to paying, they are objecting to the sequence:

> *"It's not even that I have a problem with these things being behind paywalls but rather that they ask for tons of personal stuff before displaying the price wall instead of after."* — `12325798619`

> *"Ich hasse es. Keine Testmöglichkeit… nennt doch erst den Preis oder macht die App direkt kostenpflichtig."* (I hate it. No way to try it… just state the price up front or make the app paid outright.) — `13169645512`

> *"課金しないと使えないんかい。30分かけて設定したのに課金しないと使えないんだったら初めに言って欲しい。"* (So it's unusable without paying? I spent 30 minutes setting it up — if it's unusable without paying, tell me at the start.) — `12790714776`

The identical complaint appears in Polish (`13288536663`), Spanish (`13029158735`), Portuguese (`14372121368`), French (`11946409434`), Turkish (`14022817021`), Korean (`13008212923`), Hungarian (`14049119113`), Swedish (`14278752993`), Czech (`14120767496`), Italian (`14225023215`), Dutch (`14398263738`), Arabic-script (`14407701414`) and more. **This is not a cultural or market-specific reaction. It is universal in this corpus.**

Adjacent and reinforcing:
- **`B09` advertised as free — 37 reviews (3.00%, mean 1.08★).** The store listing legitimately reads "Free with In-App Purchases"; reviewers take "free" at face value, and several cite an Instagram ad that said "get your free life reset plan today" (`14053178133`, `13679972754`, `13990396423`).
- **`B10` data collected before paywall — 20 reviews (1.62%, mean 1.10★).** A privacy framing of the same sequence. One review demands data deletion outright and compares the practice to major platforms (`12773441234`).
- **`B15` manipulative funnel — 35 reviews (2.84%, mean 1.31★).** Names specific devices: signing a pledge (`12325962990`), being made to "lock in" six times (`13168307736`), a countdown discount that does not apply (`12565582219`), and being told you are below average after entering healthy habits (`12616360983`).
- **`B16` assessment disbelieved — 13 reviews (1.06%, mean 1.38★).** Fit, disciplined reviewers told they have "40% more bad habits than the average person your age" conclude the assessment is a sales device. `12657002625`, `12568029881`, `12948658478`, `12977574466`.
- **`F10` onboarding too long — 29 reviews (2.35%, mean 1.55★), rising every era (0% → 1.3% → 2.4% → 4.1%).** Some reviewers abandon during the questionnaire itself, before any price is shown (`12849258793`, `13001541811`, `14015185909`).

### 3.4.2 Price — `B04` 113 (9.17%, mean 1.65★) and `B05` 55 (4.46%, mean 1.93★)

`B04` objects to the amount; `B05` objects to the ratio. They behave differently over time and should not be merged:

- **`B04` (too expensive) is flat-to-rising**: 6.8% → 7.6% → 10.6% → 9.3%.
- **`B05` (not worth it) is falling sharply**: 6.8% → 6.2% → 4.6% → **1.4%** (−5.4pp).

Read together, this is a good-news finding buried in a bad-news family: **reviewers increasingly accept that the product delivers, while continuing to object to what it costs.** The value objection is dying; the price objection is not.

Supporting monetization themes: `B13` prefers one-time purchase (23, 1.87%), `B14` wants a free tier, ads or donations (40, 3.25%) — several volunteering to accept heavy advertising instead (`12315674310`, `12336066250`, `12578988252`), `B11` cannot afford (30, 2.44%, of whom 12 self-identify as minors), and `B12` regional pricing and payment access (7 reviews, 0.57%, weak but specific: FX-linked pricing in Türkiye `11656304713` and `14055766092`, sanctions `11722987242`, no local payment method `14134136128` and `14147713136`).

### 3.4.3 Rigidity — the complaint of people who stayed · `E01` 80 (6.49%), `E02` 70 (5.68%) · **high-priority**

This is the most product-actionable family in the report, and its signature is unmistakable: **these reviewers rate 3–4★, not 1★**. `E01` sits at mean 3.09★ and `E02` at 2.54★, against a corpus mean of 2.77★. `E01` is the **top theme of the entire 4★ band (17.65%)** and the top theme of the 3★ band (17.12%). These are people who bought the product, want it to work, and are telling you precisely what is blocking them.

The specific failures, in descending evidence:

| Sub-problem | Theme | n | % | Mean★ | Representative IDs |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Cannot add own tasks | `E01` | 80 | 6.49% | 3.09 | `12395860314`, `12492042985`, `12532417331`, `12783646114`, `13061130125` |
| Cannot edit the generated programme | `E02` | 70 | 5.68% | 2.54 | `12330927969`, `12558857874`, `12586277596`, `12946977144`, `14501119194` |
| Night-shift / wake-time window | `E05` | 31 | 2.52% | **3.42** | `12566596906`, `12694715357`, `13008752957`, `13349247034`, `13206594751` |
| Schedule and day rigidity | `E04` | 37 | 3.00% | 2.73 | `12488374198`, `12597799240`, `12820397000`, `13072200651` |
| Task library too small | `E12` | 29 | 2.35% | 3.14 | `12382450064`, `13054611408`, `13114144584`, `13320711741` |
| Ramp unrealistic / unsafe | `E06` | 20 | 1.62% | 2.85 | `12999503079`, `13157917276`, `13312781157`, `13329009131`, `13413325683` |
| Running is the only cardio | `E03` | 20 | 1.62% | 2.75 | `12330927969`, `12356366718`, `12573130876`, `13030081397`, `13214360145` |
| Day closes / recap fires too early | `E11` | 10 | 0.81% | 3.60 | `13013314943`, `13018958577`, `13072200651`, `12988419334` |
| Disability, injury, health limits | `E08` | 10 | 0.81% | 3.10 | `12356366718`, `12370361052`, `12624578963`, `13334494888`, `13085133310` |
| Advanced user given beginner plan | `E07` | 13 | 1.06% | 3.38 | `12553771048`, `12940319320`, `12948658478`, `13111971095` |
| No pause for illness or absence | `E10` | 3 | 0.24% | 3.67 | `12879263091`, `12993334207`, `13085133310` |
| Units metric/imperial | `E09` | 3 | 0.24% | 3.67 | `12464189759`, `12613829577`, `12700784064` |

Two of these carry a safety dimension and are reported as such regardless of their share: `E06` includes reviewers describing end-state targets of 90-minute lifts plus 4-mile runs six days a week for people who were sedentary at week 1 (`13329009131`), 2 gallons of water a day (`13329009131`), 3–4 litres prescribed without assessment (`13413325683`, `14297517553`), and a licensed counsellor disputing the app's "wisdom" scoring (`12790143983`). `E08` includes asthma (`12548653005`), post-surgical injury (`12356366718`), nerve damage (`13334494888`) and mobility limits (`12370361052`) that cannot be entered. One reviewer calls the design "ableist and short-sighted" (`12370361052`).

**The counter-evidence matters too.** `E15` (8 reviews, mean 4.75★) records reviewers who found customisation had been added — `12917164876`, `13197705864`, `13310870988`, `13409263810`, `13430751290`, `13664657944`, `13894859500`, `13935807329`. All eight are from July 2025 onward, and `E01`'s era share collapses from 10.2% in E2 to **1.0% in E4**. The rigidity problem is being fixed. It is not yet fixed enough — `E02` is still 3.1% of E4 and `E05` still 1.4%.

### 3.4.4 Reliability — 195 reviews (15.83%, mean 2.38★), episodic rather than chronic

The full family is modest for a two-year-old app, but it concentrates into datable incidents (§8.3) and it hits payers nearly three times as hard as non-payers (41.5% vs 13.4%).

| Theme | n | % | Mean★ | Note |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `D18` bugs, unspecified | 48 | 3.90% | 2.60 | **Rising** — 7.9% of E4, its highest |
| `D04` progress reset or lost | 36 | 2.92% | 2.42 | 14.15% among payers |
| `D03` lag, freeze, unresponsive | 30 | 2.44% | 2.40 | Flat across eras |
| `D06` signup / onboarding blocked | 23 | 1.87% | 1.13 | The Aug–Sep 2025 name-field cluster |
| `D07` day advances wrongly | 20 | 1.62% | 2.95 | Persistent, never resolved |
| `D01` crash on launch | 20 | 1.62% | 2.10 | **Solved** — 10.2% of E1 → **0.0% of E4** |
| `D05` login / account failure | 16 | 1.30% | 1.50 | 11.32% among payers |
| `D12` device or layout incompatibility | 15 | 1.22% | 1.27 | iPad, Mac, small iPhones |
| `D13` update caused a regression | 29 | 2.35% | 3.10 | Mean 3.10★ — written by users who liked the old version |
| `D02` crash during use | 12 | 0.97% | 2.58 | Clusters in June 2026 |
| `D10` streak / XP / stat bugs | 11 | 0.89% | 3.09 | Grows with the game layer |
| `D09` notification bugs | 9 | 0.73% | 2.33 | "You haven't completed anything" after completing everything |
| `D08` widget broken | 8 | 0.65% | 3.12 | Present from 2025 to the last week of the corpus |
| `D15` restore purchase fails | 5 | 0.41% | 1.20 | **Every one is 1★.** Paid users locked out |
| `D17` character / hero mode bugs | 5 | 0.41% | 3.20 | New in 2026 only |
| `D16` dead buttons and links | 6 | 0.49% | 2.17 | Support and Discord buttons inert |
| `D11` multi-device sync | 3 | 0.24% | 2.00 | No restore between iPhone and iPad |
| `D14` meditation / audio broken | 4 | 0.32% | 1.50 | |

`D15` deserves separate mention despite 5 reviews: **all five are paying customers who cannot get back into what they bought**, four of them at 1★ (`12638305038`, `12723289227`, `12766875691`, `12932230787`, `12650990254`). Small count, maximum severity.

### 3.4.5 Product-value criticism — 204 reviews (16.56%, mean 1.96★)

| Theme | n | % | Mean★ | Trend across eras |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `F02` AI-generated content criticised | 53 | 4.30% | 1.85 | **0% → 4.4% → 4.0% → 5.5% — rising** |
| `F01` just a checklist | 38 | 3.08% | 1.68 | 1.7% → 3.1% → 4.4% → 1.0% — falling |
| `F05` do it yourself instead | 36 | 2.92% | 1.36 | Peaks in E2 at 4.9% |
| `F11` unfinished or needs polish | 36 | 2.92% | 2.64 | Flat |
| `F10` onboarding too long | 29 | 2.35% | 1.55 | **Rising to 4.1%** |
| `F04` not what the ad promised | 15 | 1.22% | 1.40 | Flat |
| `F08` tone guilt-inducing | 13 | 1.06% | 2.69 | Flat |
| `F07` no enforcement or proof | 12 | 0.97% | 1.50 | Flat |
| `F06` shallow or pseudo-science | 10 | 0.81% | 1.80 | Flat |
| `F09` narrow audience — bro-coded | 8 | 0.65% | 1.62 | Flat |
| `F03` AI personalisation valued | 6 | 0.49% | 4.50 | The counter-theme |

**`F02` is the only product criticism that rises in every era.** The objection is specifically about craft, not capability: reviewers identify AI art on task icons and achievements, AI-written motivational copy, and AI-generated book summaries, and connect it to the price — *"I cannot see myself paying full price for something that is completely built by AI… Only then can I say this is something I'd actually tell my friends about"* (`12664131139`). *"for an app promoting ideas of doing the work and not being lazy, using AI instead of just paying an artist… seems like a very cheap move"* (`12585808803`). An artist gives it 4★ anyway (`13013464738`); a developer identifies AI-generated code (`13900765197`).

Set against it, `F03` (6 reviews, mean 4.50★) shows AI *planning* is welcome — `12383006828`, `12492160331`, `12624578963`, `14057888575`, `14400104104`, `14508093211`. The distinction is sharp and actionable: **reviewers accept an AI coach and reject AI decoration.**

`F04` and `F01` together (46 distinct reviews) are the expectation gap created by the Solo Leveling marketing: people arrive expecting an RPG and find a checklist. Note that `F01` **falls to 1.0% in E4**, coinciding with the arrival of character mode.

### 3.4.6 Support — 21 reviews (1.70%, mean 1.43★)

Small in count, disproportionate in consequence. `I01` covers a support email that does not exist or 404s (`12370361052`, `12606665230`), a "contact support" control that is not a button at all (`12764415874`, `12782815053`, `13085133310`), and unanswered mail over days or weeks (`13077946636`, `13095267990`, `13326103978`, `13415116944`, `13315130235`).

**10 of the 21 are confirmed payers.** Combined with `C03` (21 reviews reporting a refund refused or ignored, mean 1.14★) and `D15` (6 payers locked out of what they bought), the picture is a support function that is hardest to reach exactly when money is involved. Against it stands `A13` — 9 reviews, mean **4.89★**, describing fast, generous handling including a refund plus a free year (`12320487953`), same-day replies (`12362756764`) and active bug recovery (`12709243457`). **Support is not incompetent; it is inconsistent and under-resourced.**

## 3.5 Unmet needs — requests, broken features, misunderstandings and price objections kept apart

The spec requires these four to be distinguished, and this corpus makes the distinction load-bearing.

**Genuine new-capability requests** (the product does not do this and no reviewer thinks it does): deeper RPG mechanics `H01` (15), more exercise types `H03` (17), incremental/over-target logging `H04` (8), themes and visual customisation `H05` (7), what-happens-after-day-66 and further programmes `H06` (5), third-party integrations `E13` (7 — Apple Health, Garmin, calendar), social/guild features `H02` (2), widget improvements `H07` (4), in-app alarm or text-to-speech `H08` (3), more content `G05` (15).

**Broken existing capability** (the feature exists and fails): the entire D family, 195 reviews. The most consequential are `D04`, `D05` and `D15`, because they destroy work the user has already done and are concentrated among payers.

**Misunderstanding or expectation mismatch** (the product is not what the reviewer was led to expect): `F04` (15) and much of `F01` (38). These are marketing artefacts, not product defects — the fix is in the ad, not the app. `B09` (37) is the monetization equivalent.

**Pricing objections** (the product is understood and the deal is rejected): `B04` (113) and `B05` (55), plus the structural objections `B06` (15) and `B13` (23). **These must not be treated as product feedback.** A reviewer who says "$18 a month is too much" is not asking for a feature.

**The largest unmet need in the corpus is not on any of these lists**: it is the ability to evaluate the product before paying. 90 reviews ask for it directly (`B03`), 40 more propose a specific alternative model (`B14`), and 331 reviews exist because it was unavailable (§3.2).

---

# PART 4 — THE PRODUCT AS SEEN BY PEOPLE WHO USED IT

The corpus mean (2.77★) is not a measure of the product, because 26.87% of it was written by people who never saw the product (§3.2). This short part restates the main findings on the honest denominator.

## 4.1 Findings recomputed on the 901 reviews that are not paywall-bounce

**Denominator:** 1,232 − 331 = **901 reviews** from people who carry at least one theme describing the product, its reliability, its content, its features, or who confirm they paid.

| Finding | Count | % of 901 | % of 1,232 | Signal on 901 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Behaviour change achieved (`A01`) | 167 | 18.54% | 13.56% | high-priority |
| Any core-praise theme (A family) | 436 | 48.39% | 35.39% | high-priority |
| Gamification loved (`A02`) | 60 | 6.66% | 4.87% | high-priority |
| Cannot add own tasks (`E01`) | 80 | 8.88% | 6.49% | high-priority |
| Cannot edit programme (`E02`) | 70 | 7.77% | 5.68% | high-priority |
| Any rigidity theme (E family) | 225 | 24.97% | 18.26% | high-priority |
| Any reliability theme (D family) | 195 | 21.64% | 15.83% | high-priority |
| AI content criticised (`F02`) | 53 | 5.88% | 4.30% | high-priority |
| Just a checklist (`F01`) | 38 | 4.22% | 3.08% | very strong |
| Night-shift / wake-time (`E05`) | 31 | 3.44% | 2.52% | very strong |

Read this way the priorities reorder. **Among people who actually used Life Reset, praise (48.4%) outweighs rigidity (25.0%) and reliability (21.6%) by a wide margin, and behaviour change alone is reported by nearly one in five.** The mean rating of these 901 reviews is 3.34★ rather than 2.77★.

This is the number to use when judging the product. The 2.77★ figure is the number to use when judging the funnel.

---

# PART 5 — RATINGS ANALYSIS

## 5.1 Distribution

| Rating | n | % of corpus | Cumulative |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 5★ | 359 | 29.14% | 29.14% |
| 4★ | 136 | 11.04% | 40.18% |
| 3★ | 111 | 9.01% | 49.19% |
| 2★ | 119 | 9.66% | 58.85% |
| 1★ | 507 | 41.15% | 100.00% |
X | **100.00%** | — |

The distribution is **bimodal and unusually so**: 41.15% at 1★ and 29.14% at 5★, with only 29.71% spread across 2–4★. This is the signature of two separate populations rather than a spectrum of opinion, and §3.1 identifies them.

## 5.2 One star — n = 507 (41.15%)

Overwhelmingly about money and sequence, not about the product working badly.

| Theme | n | % of 1★ band |
|---|---:|---:|
| `B01` hard paywall | 218 | 43.00% |
| `B02` onboarding before price | 140 | 27.61% |
| `B04` price too high | 77 | 15.19% |
| `B03` no free trial | 75 | 14.79% |
| `C01` confirmed payer | 57 | 11.24% |
| `B19` scam accusation | 40 | 7.89% |
| `B09` advertised as free | 35 | 6.90% |
| `F02` AI content criticised | 31 | 6.11% |
| `B15` manipulative funnel | 29 | 5.72% |
| `F05` do it yourself instead | 26 | 5.13% |
| `C02` refund requested | 25 | 4.93% |

**All 40 scam accusations (`B19`) sit at 1★ and nowhere else.** The word is used about the payment experience in every case — never about the app failing to function. Reviewers reach for "scam" when the price surfaces after a 20-minute investment, when a discount does not apply, or when a refund is refused.

The 57 payers inside the 1★ band are the most serious group in this report: people who gave money and then rated the lowest possible score. Their themes are `D04`/`D05` (lost progress, locked out), `C03` (refund refused), `B08` (overcharged) and `E02` (cannot change the plan they bought).

## 5.3 Two stars — n = 119 (9.66%)

The band where price and value meet. `B05` (price vs value) is the **top theme at 17.65%** — higher than in any other band — and `C01` is second at 16.81%. Two-star reviewers have usually engaged with the product, formed a view, and concluded the deal is bad. `E02` (13.45%), `D18` (11.76%) and `F01` (9.24%) fill out the picture: it works, it is rigid, it is buggy, it costs too much for what it is.

## 5.4 Three stars — n = 111 (9.01%)

**The rigidity band.** `E01` (17.12%) and `E02` (14.41%) are first and second — the only band where customisation outranks money. `F11` (unfinished, 12.61%) and `A04` (design praised, 10.81%) sit alongside. The three-star review in this corpus has a consistent shape: *the design is excellent, the idea is right, and I cannot make it fit my life.*

## 5.5 Four stars — n = 136 (11.04%)

`E01` and `A01` tie at **17.65%** — the clearest single illustration of this corpus's central tension. The modal four-star review reports genuine behaviour change **and** asks for custom tasks in the same paragraph. `D13` (update regression, 7.35%) and `E05` (night shift, 7.35%) follow. These are retained, satisfied users naming one blocker each.

## 5.6 Five stars — n = 359 (29.14%)

`A01` dominates at 38.72%, followed by `A19` generic praise (27.58%), `A02` gamification (12.26%), `A03` Solo Leveling (9.47%), `A14` worth the money (8.64%) and `A05` accountability (8.64%). `A16` (mental-health benefit) at 4.74% carries the highest mean of any theme in the taxonomy (4.94★).

Note `J09` at 4.46% of this band: 16 five-star reviews contradict their own text (§5.8).

## 5.7 Theme × rating cross-tabulation — top 28 themes

| Code | Theme | n | 1★ | 2★ | 3★ | 4★ | 5★ | Mean★ |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| B01 | Hard paywall — no free use | 256 | 218 | 16 | 10 | 4 | 8 | 1.31 |
| A01 | Behaviour change achieved | 167 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 24 | 139 | 4.78 |
| B02 | Onboarding before price disclosure | 156 | 140 | 11 | 4 | 0 | 1 | 1.15 |
| B04 | Price too high | 113 | 77 | 14 | 11 | 6 | 5 | 1.65 |
| C01 | Paid — confirmed | 106 | 57 | 20 | 15 | 9 | 5 | 1.92 |
| B03 | No free trial | 90 | 75 | 6 | 6 | 1 | 2 | 1.32 |
| E01 | Cannot add own tasks | 80 | 16 | 9 | 19 | 24 | 12 | 3.09 |
| E02 | Cannot edit or remove preset programme | 70 | 19 | 16 | 16 | 16 | 3 | 2.54 |
| A02 | Gamification / level-up loved | 60 | 1 | 1 | 6 | 8 | 44 | 4.55 |
| A04 | Design, art and UI praise | 56 | 0 | 8 | 12 | 14 | 22 | 3.89 |
| B05 | Price versus value | 55 | 23 | 21 | 4 | 6 | 1 | 1.93 |
| F02 | AI-generated content criticised | 53 | 31 | 8 | 6 | 7 | 1 | 1.85 |
| D18 | Bugs — unspecified | 48 | 13 | 14 | 6 | 9 | 6 | 2.60 |
| A07 | Motivation and encouragement | 40 | 0 | 1 | 4 | 5 | 30 | 4.60 |
| B14 | Wants a free tier, ads or donations | 40 | 19 | 13 | 3 | 4 | 1 | 1.88 |
| B19 | Scam accusation | 40 | 40 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1.00 |
| A05 | Accountability and structure | 39 | 0 | 1 | 2 | 5 | 31 | 4.69 |
| A14 | Worth the money | 39 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 6 | 31 | 4.74 |
| A03 | Solo Leveling / anime fit | 38 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 3 | 34 | 4.87 |
| F01 | Just a checklist | 38 | 22 | 11 | 1 | 3 | 1 | 1.68 |
| B09 | Advertised or listed as free | 37 | 35 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1.08 |
| E04 | Schedule and day rigidity | 37 | 9 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 4 | 2.73 |
| C02 | Refund requested | 36 | 25 | 6 | 3 | 0 | 2 | 1.56 |
| D04 | Progress reset or lost | 36 | 14 | 8 | 3 | 7 | 4 | 2.42 |
| F05 | Do it yourself instead | 36 | 26 | 7 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 1.36 |
| F11 | Unfinished or needs polish | 36 | 9 | 5 | 14 | 6 | 2 | 2.64 |
| B15 | Manipulative funnel tactics | 35 | 29 | 3 | 2 | 0 | 1 | 1.31 |
| E05 | Night shift / wake-time range | 31 | 3 | 5 | 6 | 10 | 7 | 3.42 |

Three patterns are worth naming:

- **Monetization themes are not merely negative, they are near-absolute.** `B02` has 140 of 156 at 1★. `B09` has 35 of 37. `B19` has 40 of 40. These are not opinions on a scale; they are a single reaction.
- **Rigidity themes are the opposite.** `E01` spreads 16/9/19/24/12 across the five stars and `E05` spreads 3/5/6/10/7 — genuinely mixed, skewing *positive*. A theme with a mean above 3.0★ that appears 80 times is a feature request from a retained user, not a churn driver.
- **Praise themes are extreme in the other direction.** `A03` has 34 of 38 at 5★; `A05` has zero 1★ reviews in 39; `A14` has zero 1★ or 2★ in 39.

## 5.8 Where stars and text disagree — 27 reviews (2.19%)

Flagged as `J09`, never re-scored. Two directions:

The split is 16 at 5★, 2 at 4★, 2 at 2★ and 7 at 1★.

**Five stars, complaint or non-endorsement text** (16 reviews). `12808632076` is titled *"THIS is actually a one star review. I just wanted you to see it"* — a deliberate inversion to gain visibility. `12970786786` is a 5★ review whose entire body is "환불을 원합니다" (I want a refund). `12590518185` is 5★ from someone who could not afford it and never used it; `12410734128` is 5★ from a 16-year-old asking for a free subscription; `14468052541` is 5★ arguing the developer should change the pricing model; `12597880797` is 5★ whose only content is that night shifts are unsupported.

**One and two stars, praise or conditional text** (9 reviews). `11830839391` is 4★ titled "Crushes right after start". `12684869518` is 1★ saying *"I am enjoying the app and will change the review once they fix it"* — explicit rating-as-leverage. `13036014047` states the mechanism outright: *"Gunna remove the one Star as soon as I know you got the message it's just to get your attention."* `13329009131` is 2★ titled "Great App". `13407568757` is 1★ from a user who says it used to be five stars and documents exactly which update changed their mind.

**Implication:** a non-trivial number of App Store ratings for this app are being used as a signalling channel rather than as an evaluation. Any analysis that reads ratings alone will misclassify at least these 27, and probably more.

---

# PART 6 — PAID-USER AND UPGRADE ANALYSIS

## 6.1 Who is identifiable as a payer

**106 reviews (8.60% of the corpus) contain first-person evidence of paying** — "I paid", "I bought the year", "I subscribed", "it charged me", or an account of being a specified number of days into a programme that only payment unlocks. This is a **floor on paid reviewers, not a conversion rate** (§1.6).

| Rating | n | % of payer segment |
|---|---:|---:|
| 5★ | 5 | 4.72% |
| 4★ | 9 | 8.49% |
| 3★ | 15 | 14.15% |
| 2★ | 20 | 18.87% |
| 1★ | 57 | 53.77% |
| **Total** | **106** | mean **1.92★** |

**This is a complaint sample, not a satisfaction sample, and the mechanism is visible in the data.** 91.5% of payer reviews (97 of 106) carry at least one complaint theme, while only 15.1% (16) carry any praise. Satisfied payers overwhelmingly wrote short, warm reviews that never mention money — which is exactly why `A14` ("worth the money", 39 reviews, mean 4.74★) has only partial overlap with `C01`. The payer segment should be read as **an inventory of what goes wrong after the money changes hands**, and it is a good one.

## 6.2 What triggers a purchase — in buyers' own words

The corpus supports four distinct purchase triggers, each with direct evidence:

1. **The Solo Leveling / anime frame.** The single most-named trigger. `13675335199`: *"I was thinking about it after watching solo leveling on repeat. I wanted to level up like jinwoo sung and came across this app through an ad."* Also `12634140436`, `13441364129`, `13713859618`, `14025544799`; see `A03` in §10.8.
2. **A social ad plus emotional timing.** 15 reviews (`J08`) name Instagram or TikTok. `12937028933` is disarmingly explicit: *"I saw it on insta at 2am and got the 2am motivation to change my life so now I have a year of this app."* Other reviewers describe watching the anime and buying immediately after (`12702465493`, `13675335199`, `14497623684`).
3. **The onboarding itself, working as designed.** Several buyers say the questionnaire and the personalised result are what convinced them, which is the funnel functioning as intended for the segment it fits: `14400545320`, `14508093211`, `14057888575`.
4. **Being at a low point.** A distinct and repeated framing. `13447663494`: *"This requires you to be at the lowest point in life… if you seriously have no motivation or self control get this app."* Also `13143280221` (severe depression), `13218727034` (Polish: two years lost, zero motivation), `14515700777` (moved country, lost all habits), `13751654351`.

The discount mechanic converts, and it also generates the `B07` complaints: `13198338741` describes chasing an 80% notification that resolves to 70%, and buyers describe the "limited time offer" as the thing that tipped them (`12890488389`, `13930327076`).

## 6.3 Post-purchase failures — where paying customers get hurt

Themes over-represented among payers versus the global corpus. Segment rates are shown as segment rates, alongside the global count, per the spec.

| Theme | Payer segment (n=106) | Global (n=1,232) | Δ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `E02` cannot edit the programme | 16 (15.09%) | 70 (5.68%) | +9.41pp |
| `D04` progress reset or lost | 15 (14.15%) | 36 (2.92%) | **+11.23pp** |
| `B05` price versus value | 15 (14.15%) | 55 (4.46%) | +9.69pp |
| `D05` login / account failure | 12 (11.32%) | 16 (1.30%) | **+10.02pp** |
| `D18` bugs, unspecified | 12 (11.32%) | 48 (3.90%) | +7.42pp |
| `E01` cannot add own tasks | 10 (9.43%) | 80 (6.49%) | +2.94pp |
| `E04` schedule rigidity | 10 (9.43%) | 37 (3.00%) | +6.43pp |
| `F01` just a checklist | 10 (9.43%) | 38 (3.08%) | +6.35pp |
| `I01` support unreachable | 10 (9.43%) | 21 (1.70%) | +7.73pp |
| `B08` overcharged / double-charged | 9 (8.49%) | 9 (0.73%) | **+7.76pp** |
| `D03` lag, freeze, unresponsive | 9 (8.49%) | 30 (2.44%) | +6.06pp |
| `B07` pricing misleading at checkout | 8 (7.55%) | 17 (1.38%) | +6.17pp |

**Every single `B08` review in the corpus is from a payer.** That is definitionally near-certain but worth stating: nine reviewers describe being charged an amount other than the one displayed — €1.99/month becoming €30 at once (`12517809398`), $2/month becoming $70 (`12559596166`), £25 charged on top of £29.99 (`13083371537`), a year charged during a trial (`13668928014`), and seven months of charges continuing after cancellation (`14518998814`).

Three failure clusters account for most of the damage:

- **Losing the work.** `D04` + `D05` + `D15` = 48 distinct reviews, of which **24 are payers**. The pattern is consistent: the user is 4 to 46 days into a 66-day programme, an update or a logout occurs, and the programme restarts at day 1. `13173016236` loses 46 of 66 days. `14116238467` loses a 177-day streak. `12766742930`, `12766804328`, `12766865971`, `12766875691`, `12766884799` and `12766887680` lose accounts within 48 hours of each other in June 2025.
- **Not being able to change what you bought.** `E01` + `E02` among payers = 23 distinct reviews. Several state the sequence plainly: paid, discovered the plan is fixed, requested a refund (`12330927969`, `12370361052`, `13228639633`, `14501119194`).
- **Not being able to reach anyone.** `I01` + `C03` = 36 distinct reviews, **20 of them payers**.

## 6.4 Refunds, cancellations and churn

| Theme | n | % of corpus | Of whom payers | Mean★ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `C02` refund requested | 36 | 2.92% | 26 | 1.56 |
| `C03` refund denied or ignored | 21 | 1.70% | 15 | 1.14 |
| `C04` cancelled or churned | 20 | 1.62% | 10 | 1.95 |
| `C05` money-back guarantee referenced | 13 | 1.06% | 6 | 1.85 |

**The refund experience is the corpus's most acute reputational liability.** 21 of 36 refund requests (58.3%) end in refusal or silence, at a mean of 1.14★ — the second-lowest mean of any theme. Reviewers describe emailing for days (`13077946636`, `13326103978`), being told the app is not eligible for an App Store refund and then hitting a 404 on App Support (`12370361052`), and cancelling a subscription that kept billing (`14518998814`).

**The "66-day money-back guarantee" is doing damage rather than reassurance.** It is marketed prominently enough that 13 reviewers cite it, and at least four describe conditions they say were not disclosed: `13045702582` reports the guarantee requires 66 logged days at 50%+ task completion — *"Very misleading"*; `14203716713` calls it out by name in the title; `14043796481` (German) says every condition was met and no refund arrived; `13346302196` (Japanese) describes a PayPal transfer confirmed by email that never landed after more than a week. `13562999761` says the guarantee is "nowhere to be found".

This is a case where a marketing promise is generating one-star reviews at a higher rate than having no promise would.

## 6.5 Barriers to upgrading — people who could have paid and did not

The largest group in the corpus (§3.2) never reached a decision about value at all, because they could not see the product. Beyond them, four specific barriers are evidenced:

1. **No way to evaluate.** `B03`, 90 reviews. The argument is usually commercial rather than emotional: `12763600489` — *"An obvious approach would be to allow the first week to run freely. Get the user on a run, and they'll pay to continue."* `14468052541` makes the same case from a developer's perspective. `13000976164`, `13484215008` and `13309060596` all propose a specific trial length.
2. **Plan structure.** `B06`, 15 reviews. A 66-day programme sold as a 12-month subscription is the recurring objection (`12226199321`, `12320820692`, `13447411167`), sharpened where the monthly plan costs more for two months than the annual does for twelve (`12320820692`, `12597933037`).
3. **Affordability, concentrated in a young audience.** `B11`, 30 reviews, of which **12 self-identify as minors** (`11640307135`, `11785674594`, `12410734128`, `12429383039`, `12544612652`, `12758322831`, `12812223251`, `13017744517`, `13256564481`, `13329551903`, `13721531038`, `14033270488`). The app is rated 9+ and marketed on anime social media; the mismatch between audience and price point is structural, not incidental. The under-18 free programme (`J02`, 3 reviews) appears to address this but is invisible to almost everyone.
4. **Regional price and payment access.** `B12`, 7 reviews (0.57%, weak signal, but unambiguous): dollar-pegged pricing in Türkiye (`11656304713`, `14055766092`), sanctions blocking cards (`11722987242`), no local payment method (`14134136128` asking for UPI, `14147713136`).

## 6.6 The trial experiment — the corpus's clearest natural experiment

A free trial was absent for most of the window, then introduced. The evidence for the date is `B18`: **zero occurrences before 24 January 2026, eleven after** (`13668928014`, `13887104942`, `13991693711`, `13996564291`, `14031955343`, `14203716713`, `14303233696`, `14309471713`, `14413359762`, `14504421154`, `14508328179`). `C06` (trial used) matches: 2 mentions in late 2024, none at all for 15 months, then 7 from March 2026.

Comparing the corpus either side of 24 January 2026:

| Theme | Before (n=986) | From 24 Jan 2026 (n=246) | Change |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `B01` hard paywall | 170 (17.24%) | 86 (**34.96%**) | +17.72pp |
| `B02` onboarding before price | 117 (11.87%) | 39 (15.85%) | +3.98pp |
| `B09` advertised as free | 21 (2.13%) | 16 (6.50%) | +4.37pp |
| `B03` no free trial | 69 (7.00%) | 21 (8.54%) | +1.54pp |
| `B18` trial charged immediately | 0 (0.00%) | 11 (4.47%) | +4.47pp |
| `C06` trial used | 2 (0.20%) | 7 (2.85%) | +2.65pp |
| Mean rating | 2.77★ | 2.78★ | +0.01 |

**The trial did not solve the problem it was introduced to solve.** Paywall complaints roughly doubled as a share; "advertised as free" tripled; and a new billing-complaint theme appeared from nothing. The mean rating did not move.

The reviews explain why. The trial requires a card and charges at once or at expiry without effective warning: `14309471713` — *"I signed up for the 1 week free trial and was immediately charged AU\$17.98."* `13668928014` — *"I signed up for a trial and was immediately charged for one year. It does not show up in your Apple subscriptions and there is no way to cancel your trial!"* `14303233696` cancelled a day early and was still charged \$50. `13996564291` describes a loop: *"They made a loop so you can not use the app if you don't accept the free trial, no way you bypass it."* `14504421154` (German) reports the 7-day trial applies only to the annual plan and was billed €14.99 immediately.

**Causation is not established** (§1.6): a concurrent advertising push could have brought in a less-qualified audience, and the 2026 sample is 246 reviews. But the direction is consistent across four independent themes, and the mechanism is described directly by eleven reviewers. The safe reading is: **a card-gated auto-charging trial with an unclear exit has the reputational cost of a paywall plus the reputational cost of a billing surprise.**

## 6.7 Competitive position as reviewers describe it

15 reviews (`A10`, mean 4.80★) say Life Reset beat everything else they tried. Named rivals appear on both sides: reviewers recommend **Me+**, **Finch** (`12566560611`), **Atoms** by James Clear (`12540429847` — cited approvingly as the model for customisation), **Habitica** (`13413661335` — *"it's just a stylized version of Habitica but with cards"*), **Habit Legends** (`14195130483` — *"no subscriptions, keeps you pulled in with a story line and MUCH MUCH more flexible with editing tasks"*), **Level-Up** (`12546377805` — *"At least it's free"*), **Opal** (`12831447653`) and **Notion** templates (`12439837862`, `13933722304`, `14510790013`).

The competitive threat named most often is not another habit app — it is **ChatGPT and free alternatives**. `F05` (36 reviews) repeatedly argues the plan can be generated free (`12582558677`, `13022214124`, `13776867685`, `14446491169`). One reviewer says they will rebuild it themselves on Replit (`13228144295`).

**The defensible position the corpus points to is the one reviewers actually name**: the level-up loop, the art direction, the community, and the fact that it removes the daily decision. None of those is reproducible with a prompt. The checklist is.

## 7.2 United States — n = 579 (47.00%), mean 2.97★

The US is nearly half the corpus, and it is **above the global mean** (2.97 vs 2.77) with the highest 5★ share of any large storefront (32.8%).

| Code | Theme | n | Country share | Global share | Δ | Country signal |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |

| B01 | Hard paywall — no free use | 92 | 15.89% | 20.78% | -4.89pp | high-priority |
| B02 | Onboarding before price disclosure | 56 | 9.67% | 12.66% | -2.99pp | high-priority |
| A19 | Generic praise | 54 | 9.33% | 8.85% | +0.48pp | high-priority |
| B04 | Price too high | 49 | 8.46% | 9.17% | -0.71pp | high-priority |

| A02 | Gamification / level-up loved | 34 | 5.87% | 4.87% | +1.00pp | high-priority |
| E02 | Cannot edit or remove preset programme | 30 | 5.18% | 5.68% | -0.50pp | high-priority |
| F02 | AI-generated content criticised | 27 | 4.66% | 4.30% | +0.36pp | very strong |
| E05 | Night shift / wake-time range | 27 | 4.66% | 2.52% | +2.15pp | very strong |
| A14 | Worth the money | 25 | 4.32% | 3.17% | +1.15pp | very strong |

| A07 | Motivation and encouragement | 25 | 4.32% | 3.25% | +1.07pp | very strong |

**Reading:** the US is where the product is most loved and least funnel-damaged. `A01` (behaviour change) runs at **18.13%, +4.58pp above global** — the largest positive deviation of any theme in any eligible storefront. `B01` runs **4.89pp below** global and `B02` **2.99pp below**. Two themes over-index negatively: `E05` (night shift, 4.66%, +2.15pp) — a US-specific artefact of shift-work prevalence and the reason §9.1 ranks that fix so highly — and `F05` (do it yourself, +1.05pp), consistent with a market comfortable substituting ChatGPT.

The US supplies 27 of the 31 night-shift reviews and 105 of the 167 behaviour-change reviews. **Any global percentage in this report is substantially a US percentage**, which §7.10 quantifies.

Its distinguishing deviations are `E01` (+3.31pp), `E03` (running as the only cardio, 4.90% vs 1.62% — **+3.28pp**, the strongest over-index for that theme anywhere) and `B03` (+2.50pp). Canadian reviewers also complain about local pricing in absolute terms — `12912286561` itemises CA\$79.99 or CA\$24.99 plus 14.99% Quebec tax. `A04` (design praise) over-indexes at +3.30pp, so this is a market that likes the craft and rejects the constraints.

**Reading:** the most rigidity-focused storefront in the corpus. `E02` runs at **15.38%, +9.70pp above global** — by far the largest deviation of any theme in any eligible market — and `E01` at +4.50pp. `B03` (no free trial) is +8.08pp; German reviewers ask for a *Probezeitraum* or *Testphase* repeatedly and explicitly (`12551087382` asks for one or two weeks; `13169645512`, `13301110278`, `13288916145`, `14413153828`). `F08` (guilt-inducing tone) over-indexes at +3.34pp — `13092628844` gives the fullest statement in the corpus of why the escalating-target design demotivates someone who wanted habit *stability* rather than progression.

Germany is also where localisation lands: `12634875883` notes German arriving in a May 2025 update; `12783916760` gives a five-point improvement list including that the German is *"sprachlich nicht ganz sauber"*; `14436099765` and `14437535969` still ask for German book summaries and courses in 2026.

**Reading:** the lowest mean of any eligible storefront, and the most **transaction-damaged**. `C01` (confirmed payers) is 13.92%, **+5.32pp** — the UK has the highest payer density of any eligible market — and it pairs with `B07` (misleading checkout pricing) at **8.86%, +7.48pp**, `C02` (refund requested) at +4.67pp and `B15` (manipulative funnel) at +3.49pp. The specific complaint recurs: an advertised £1.99–£2/month resolves to a £24.99–£29.99 annual charge at checkout (`12403932813`, `12458852234`, `12591354618`, `13083371537`, `12315420170`).

This is the clearest market-level evidence that the checkout presentation, not the price level, is what is generating one-star reviews. `B04` (price too high) actually runs **below** global here (−2.84pp).
