## 7.7 Poland, Japan, South Korea, India — limited evidence, but distinctive

All four fall below 50 reviews. **No standalone conclusion is drawn from them**; they are included because each shows a pattern that is coherent and that a larger sample should test.

| Storefront | n | Mean★ | Pattern |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Poland | 30 | **2.00** | The lowest mean of any storefront with 10+ reviews. `B02` appears in 13 of 30 (43.3%) — the highest paywall-sequence concentration anywhere. Yet Poland also produced four of the corpus's most enthusiastic long reviews (`13203380781`, `13218727034`, `13253630482`, `13278191408`), all 5★, all 2+ upvotes. Polarised, not negative. |
| Japan | 31 | 2.77 | Reliability-weighted: `D02` in 3, `D01`/`D18` present. The complaint register is distinctive — `13008944544` abandons on text volume alone, `13001541811` on the "religious" framing of the intro. Localisation of book summaries and meditation is asked for repeatedly (`13424071995`, `13346302196`, `13470008376`). One reviewer (`13481941295`) objects to the 2025 female-character art as gratuitous. |
| South Korea | 22 | 3.05 | **Translation-weighted**: `G02` in 4 of 22 (18.2%), the highest anywhere. `13008212923` gives the fullest account — unnatural translation, English-only reading feature, no trial, no refund. `E01` in 5 of 22. |
| India | 20 | 2.85 | Bimodal: 50% 1★ and 45% 5★, almost nothing between. `B01` in 7; behaviour-change praise in 4. `13888253741` writes the corpus's clearest freemium proposal (3–5 free habits, premium above). |

## 7.8 High-spend market group

**Membership is declared, not inferred from this corpus.** The group is the ten largest consumer-spend App Store markets as consistently reported by app-analytics league tables (Sensor Tower / data.ai class sources): **US, JP, GB, DE, CA, AU, KR, FR, CN, TW**. No spend figure is claimed for this app; the group is a market classification only.

| | n | % of corpus | Mean★ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| High-spend group | 980 | 79.55% | 2.83 |
| Rest of world | 252 | 20.45% | 2.56 |

The high-spend group rates **0.27★ higher** than the rest of the world. The gap is driven by the US (2.97) and is partly offset inside the group by France (1.93, n=15) and Germany (2.53).

**What this does and does not mean.** It means the markets where paid apps usually perform best are also where this corpus is least hostile. It does **not** mean revenue is concentrated there — this corpus contains no revenue data, and `C01` density varies more by *grievance* than by market (the UK's 13.92% payer share reflects unhappy payers, not more payers).

The group's distinctive friction is not price but transaction integrity: `B07` and `B08` are concentrated in GB, DE, CA and US.

## 7.9 High-review-volume market group

**A disclosed volume proxy only. These are review counts, not downloads, and must never be described as downloads.** The group is this corpus's own top ten storefronts by written-review count: **US, CA, DE, GB, AU, JP, PL, KR, IN, MX**.

| | n | % of corpus | Mean★ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| High-volume group | 1,028 | 83.44% | 2.82 |
| Rest of world | 204 | 16.56% | 2.54 |

Eight of the ten overlap with the high-spend group. The two that differ — **Poland (2.00★)** and **Mexico (2.88★)** — are instructive: Poland is the corpus's angriest sizeable market and Mexico one of its warmer ones, and neither appears in the spend group. Review volume and market spend are not interchangeable proxies here, which is why both groups are reported separately.

## 7.10 Global versus country comparison

| Theme | Global | US | CA | DE | GB | AU | Spread |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `B01` hard paywall | 20.78% | 15.89% | 14.71% | 19.78% | 17.72% | 15.52% | 6.1pp |
| `B02` onboarding before price | 12.66% | 9.67% | 14.71% | 14.29% | 13.92% | 6.90% | 7.8pp |
| `B03` no free trial | 7.31% | — | 9.80% | **15.38%** | 13.92% | 8.62% | — |
| `B04` price too high | 9.17% | 8.46% | 10.78% | 13.19% | 6.33% | 5.17% | 8.0pp |
| `A01` behaviour change | 13.56% | **18.13%** | 12.75% | 7.69% | — | 13.79% | 10.4pp |
| `E01` cannot add tasks | 6.49% | 6.91% | 9.80% | 10.99% | 10.13% | 10.34% | 4.1pp |
| `E02` cannot edit programme | 5.68% | 5.18% | 6.86% | **15.38%** | 8.86% | — | 10.2pp |
| `F02` AI criticised | 4.30% | 4.66% | 5.88% | — | — | **10.34%** | 5.7pp |
| `C01` confirmed payer | 8.60% | 7.77% | 9.80% | 10.99% | **13.92%** | 10.34% | 6.2pp |

(Dashes indicate the theme is outside that storefront's top 14 and is not separately tabulated; §10.8 holds the complete per-theme ID lists from which any cell can be recomputed.)

**Three conclusions:**

1. **The paywall grievance is genuinely universal.** `B01` varies only 14.71%–19.78% across five markets on three continents, and appears in 20-plus languages. This is not a localisation or pricing-tier problem that a regional fix would address.
2. **The praise is not universal — it is American.** `A01` spans 7.69% (DE) to 18.13% (US), the widest spread of any theme. Either the product fits US routines better, or the US is further into its adoption curve, or the German-language experience is materially worse. The `G01`/`G02` evidence (§3.4) supports the third at least partly.
3. **Rigidity is a European complaint and AI is an antipodean one.** `E02` is 15.38% in Germany against 5.18% in the US; `F02` is 10.34% in Australia against 4.30% globally. Both are actionable and neither would surface from a US-only read.

## 7.11 Sub-50 storefronts — limited evidence only

Seventy storefronts hold fewer than 50 reviews each (323 reviews, 26.22%). **No standalone claim is made about any of them.** Their reviews are included in every global calculation.

Three observations are recorded as *limited evidence* because they are materially useful and well-supported within their own small samples:

- **Hong Kong (n=10, mean 4.20★, 80% 5★)** is the most positive storefront with more than 5 reviews, and 7 of its 10 reviews carry `A01`. With n=10 this is an observation, not a finding.
- **Non-English localisation gaps cluster predictably.** `G01` (16 reviews) spans DE, BR, KR, JP, ES, AR, UY and US; `G02` (10) spans KR, DE, JP, ES. Every review asking for translated book summaries or meditations comes from a non-English storefront. The signal is weak globally (1.30% / 0.81%) but coherent.
- **Several one- and two-review storefronts are entirely 1★** (Bosnia & Herzegovina, Croatia, Bahrain, Finland, Iceland, China, El Salvador, Bolivia, Yemen, Venezuela, Luxembourg, Morocco, Portugal). With n≤2 this carries no information and is listed only so that §7.1's table is not misread.

---

# PART 8 — TIME-TREND ANALYSIS

## 8.1 Method

The corpus spans 29 months and is heavily back-loaded, so **fixed calendar years are useless here** (2024 has 52 reviews, 2025 has 910). Four eras were defined from the corpus's own volume and incident shape, not from a calendar:

| Era | Window | n | % | Mean★ | 1★ share | 5★ share |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| E1 pre-scale | 2024-04-16 → 2025-01-31 | 59 | 4.79% | 2.59 | 42.4% | 22.0% |
| E2 scale-up | 2025-02-01 → 2025-06-30 | 384 | 31.17% | 2.61 | 40.9% | 21.1% |
| E3 mid | 2025-07-01 → 2025-11-30 | 499 | 40.50% | 2.86 | 39.7% | 32.1% |
| E4 recent | 2025-12-01 → 2026-09-07 | 290 | 23.54% | 2.87 | 43.8% | 36.2% |

- **E1 pre-scale** — from first review to the end of January 2025. Thin, pre-marketing-push, product called Rise.
- **E2 scale-up** — February to June 2025. Volume arrives; the May 2025 crash wave and the June 2025 logout wave fall here.
- **E3 mid** — July to November 2025. Peak volume; feature build-out (journal, customisation, screen blocker); the Aug–Sep signup-blocked cluster.
- **E4 recent** — December 2025 onward. Character/hero mode; the trial appears on 24 January 2026.

Every trend below is stated as a **share of its era**, never as a count, because era sizes differ by a factor of eight. Trends are labelled by confidence, and a trend is not claimed where the underlying counts cannot bear it.

## 8.3 Theme movement across eras

## 8.4 Trend 1 — The product got better and users noticed · **high confidence**

`A01` (behaviour change achieved) goes **6.8% → 6.8% → 16.8% → 18.3%**, a +11.5pp move and the largest of any theme in the corpus. `A02` (gamification) goes 0.0% → 2.6% → 6.2% → 6.6%. `A03` (Solo Leveling fit) goes 0.0% → 2.1% → 3.4% → 4.5%. `A12` (developer responsiveness) goes 0.0% → 1.0% → 1.4% → 2.8%. The 5★ share of each era rises 22.0% → 21.1% → 32.1% → 36.2%.

Simultaneously the value objection collapses: `B05` (price versus value) falls **6.8% → 6.2% → 4.6% → 1.4%**.

**Interpretation:** the 2025 build-out worked. People who get into the product increasingly report that it does what it claims, and increasingly stop arguing that it is not worth the money. This is the strongest positive signal in the report and it is corroborated from two independent directions (praise rising, value objection falling).

## 8.5 Trend 2 — The funnel grievance did not improve, and in E4 it worsened · **high confidence**

`B01` goes 27.1% → 19.8% → 14.4% → **31.7%**. `B02` goes 6.8% → 12.8% → 12.4% → 14.1%. `B09` ("advertised as free") goes 3.4% → 2.3% → 1.8% → **5.9%**. `F10` (onboarding too long) rises every era: 0.0% → 1.3% → 2.4% → **4.1%**. The "never used it" cohort is **33.1% of E4**, its highest.

The E3 dip in `B01` (14.4%) is the product working: E3 is when praise volume rose, diluting the funnel share. The E4 spike is not dilution reversing — it is an absolute change coinciding with the trial's introduction (§8.6).

**Interpretation:** every improvement in this corpus happened behind the paywall, where most reviewers never got. The gap between product quality and first impression widened rather than closed.

## 8.6 Trend 3 — The trial launched and made the complaint worse · **medium-high confidence**

Covered in full in §6.6. In era terms: `B18` (trial charged immediately) is **0 reviews in E1, E2 and E3, and 11 in E4 (3.8%)**; `C06` (trial used) is 3.4% / 0.0% / 0.0% / 2.4%. Across the exact 24 January 2026 boundary, `B01` goes 17.24% → 34.96% and `B09` 2.13% → 6.50%.

**Confidence is medium-high rather than high** because the post-boundary sample is 246 reviews, a concurrent ad campaign cannot be ruled out from this data, and the trial's exact launch date is inferred from first complaint rather than observed. The direction is nonetheless consistent across four themes and is described mechanically by eleven reviewers.

## 8.7 Trend 4 — Crashes were fixed; a different kind of bug replaced them · **high confidence**

`D01` (crash on launch) goes **10.2% → 2.9% → 0.6% → 0.0%**. It is genuinely solved: not one review in the 290-review E4 era reports the app failing to open. `D05` (login failure) goes 3.4% → 2.3% → 0.8% → 0.3%. `D06` (signup blocked) goes 3.4% → 1.0% → 3.2% → 0.3%.

Against that, `D18` (unspecified bugs) goes 5.1% → 2.1% → 2.8% → **7.9%**, its highest ever, and `D17` (character/hero mode bugs) appears for the first time in E4 at 1.7%. `D13` (update caused a regression) never falls below 1.6% after E1.

**Interpretation:** the platform stabilised and the feature surface destabilised. The bugs of 2026 are in the game layer — lost loot, broken stat tracking, tasks pre-checked, streaks resetting — rather than in the app's ability to launch. That is a better class of problem, but `D18` at 7.9% says it is now more common.

## 8.8 Trend 5 — Rigidity was substantially fixed · **high confidence**

`E01` (cannot add own tasks) goes 1.7% → **10.2%** → 7.4% → **1.0%**. `E02` goes 3.4% → 7.6% → 6.0% → 3.1%. `E06` (ramp unrealistic) goes 0.0% → 1.3% → 2.6% → 0.7%. `E05` (night shift) goes 0.0% → 2.9% → 3.2% → 1.4%. Meanwhile `E15` (customisation explicitly noted as improved) appears only from July 2025 onward, 8 reviews, mean 4.75★.

This is the second-clearest improvement in the corpus after the crash fix, and the corroborating review is unusually direct: `12917164876` (Indonesia, July 2025) is a complete before/after account from a user who abandoned the app over rigidity in April 2025 and returned to find it fixed.

**But it is not finished.** `E02` remains at 3.1% of E4 and `E05` at 1.4%, and the most recent rigidity reviews are still specific: `14501119194` (Italy, Sept 2026) — *"Pochissime possibilità di modificare e gestire le proprie scelte"*; `13114144584` (Canada) — *"All the tasks are pre-made so it's not customizable."*

## 8.9 Trend 6 — The AI objection is the one criticism that keeps growing · **high confidence**

`F02` goes **0.0% → 4.4% → 4.0% → 5.5%**. It is the only theme in the Product-value family that is higher in E4 than in E2. It is also spreading beyond art into copy and code: E2 reviews name AI images (`12401792876`, `12585808803`); E4 reviews name the writing itself (`14503981277` — *"The text is replete with the odd linguistic signatures characteristic of an LLM"*), the typos (`13827463222`), and the code (`13900765197`, from a reviewer identifying as a developer).

**Interpretation:** as the audience matures and AI detection becomes culturally routine, this objection gets cheaper to make and harder to ignore. It attaches specifically to the price: reviewers are unwilling to pay premium prices for assets they believe cost nothing.

## 8.10 Trend 7 — Reliability incidents are datable and each one is visible in the ratings

Five windows, each identified from the corpus rather than from a release log:

| Window | Dates | Window n | Concentration | Window mean★ |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|
| Launch-crash cluster | 2024-10-11 → 2024-11-10 | 16 | `D01` in 37.5% of window vs 1.62% corpus | 2.19 |
| **Update crash wave** | 2025-05-22 → 2025-06-05 | 45 | `D01`+`D13` in 37.8% vs 3.73% corpus | 2.82 |
| Logout / account wave | 2025-06-10 → 2025-06-20 | 39 | `D05`+`D04` in 20.5% vs 3.81% corpus | **1.92** |
| Signup-blocked cluster | 2025-08-01 → 2025-09-30 | 234 | `D06`+`D12` in 6.4% vs 2.27% corpus | 2.85 |
| Crash-on-complete | 2026-06-01 → 2026-06-30 | 28 | `D02`+`D18` in 32.1% vs 4.46% corpus | 2.39 |

The **22 May 2025 update** is the most instructive. Eleven reviews dated 22–23 May say the app stopped opening entirely, and they are written by engaged users mid-programme: day 39 (`12686272669`), day 20 (`12686231087`), 17 days consistent (`12684208109`), halfway through (`12685922018`, `12687016151`). Several are 4–5★ reviews from people warning rather than condemning — which is why the window mean (2.82) is *above* the corpus mean despite an app that would not launch. `12704616693` (Germany) adds that the same update changed the product concept, not just broke it.

The **June 2025 logout wave** is the most damaging: six accounts lost inside 48 hours (`12766742930`, `12766804328`, `12766865971`, `12766875691`, `12766884799`, `12766887680`), all payers or near-payers, window mean **1.92★**.

## 8.11 Trend 8 — What did not change in 29 months

Three things are flat across all four eras and should be read as structural rather than circumstantial:

- **`D07` (day advances wrongly)** — 3.4% / 1.0% / 1.8% / 1.7%. Reported continuously from October 2024 (`11798790357`) to June 2026 (`14216058151`). Twenty reviews, never fixed.
- **`D08` (widget broken)** — present in March 2025 (`12471607720`), May 2025 (`12604750095`), November 2025 (`13430751290`), December 2025 (`13562999761`) and the final week of the corpus (`14519880651`). Eight reviews across 18 months describing the same blank or stale widget.
- **The 1–2★ share** — 55.8% of 2024, 50.7% of 2025, 50.4% of 2026. Half of everyone who writes about this app writes a negative review, and that has not moved despite every product improvement documented above.

---

# PART 9 — PRODUCT IMPLICATIONS

Every recommendation names the findings that support it. Immediate fixes are separated from structural changes, experiments and open questions.

## 9.1 Immediate fixes — the evidence is unambiguous

**1. Disclose the price before the questionnaire, not after it.**
*Supports:* §3.4.1 (`B02` 156, 12.66%, mean **1.15★** — the lowest mean of any substantial theme), `B09` (37), `B10` (20), `B15` (35), `F10` (29); §3.2 (the 331-review cohort); §7.10 (universal across five markets and 20+ languages).
This is the single highest-value change available and it costs one screen. A large group of these reviewers say explicitly that they do not object to paying — they object to learning about it after 10–20 minutes of personal disclosure (`12325798619`, `13169645512`, `12790714776`, `14474296325`). A price shown on the first screen converts a one-star review into a non-download.

**2. Make the trial a real trial, or remove it.**
*Supports:* §6.6 (the 24 January 2026 natural experiment), `B18` (11), `B03` (90), `B09` (+4.37pp after launch), `B01` (+17.72pp after launch).
The current implementation charges at signup or at expiry without an exit users can find (`13668928014`, `14309471713`, `14303233696`, `13996564291`, `14504421154`). It has produced a new complaint category without reducing the old one. Either gate it behind no card and let the first week run free — which is precisely what reviewers propose (`12763600489`, `13000976164`, `13309060596`, `14468052541`) — or drop it and price honestly up front. The half-measure is the worst of the three options.

**3. Widen the wake-time window to cover night shifts.**
*Supports:* §3.4.3 (`E05` 31, 2.52%, mean **3.42★**; `J03` 23), §7.2 (US over-index +2.15pp).
The cheapest high-value fix in the report. These reviewers *want the product* — their mean rating is well above the corpus — and they are being excluded by a form control that permits roughly 5am–10am. Twenty-three of them state their shift pattern explicitly (`12566596906`, `13008752957`, `13244224102`, `13349247034`, `13260589327`, `13196963353`, `13198947881`). One reviewer who got a partial fix upgraded 4★ → 5★ in the review itself (`13935807329`).

**4. Answer paying customers, and fix the support entry points.**
*Supports:* §3.4.6 (`I01` 21, mean 1.43★, **10 payers**), §6.4 (`C03` 21, mean 1.14★, 15 payers), `D16` (6 dead buttons).
Three separate reviewers report the "contact support" control is not a button (`12764415874`, `12782815053`, `13085133310`); one reports App Support resolving to a 404 (`12370361052`); several report multi-day silence (`13077946636`, `13326103978`, `13415116944`). Against this, `A13` shows that when support does respond it is outstanding (mean **4.89★**, including a refund plus a free year in `12320487953`). The capability exists; the routing and the staffing do not.

**5. Stop losing people's programmes.**
*Supports:* §6.3 (`D04` 14.15% of payers, `D05` 11.32%, `D15` all five are payers), §8.10 (the June 2025 logout wave, window mean 1.92★).
Losing 46 of 66 days (`13173016236`) or a 177-day streak (`14116238467`) destroys the exact asset the product is selling. Restore-purchase failure (`D15`) is the most severe five-review theme in the corpus: paying customers locked out of what they bought.

**6. Clarify or withdraw the 66-day money-back guarantee.**
*Supports:* §6.4 (`C05` 13, mean 1.85★; `C03` 21).
Four reviewers describe conditions they say were undisclosed — 66 logged days at ≥50% completion (`13045702582`), met conditions with no payment (`14043796481`), a confirmed PayPal transfer that never arrived (`13346302196`), a guarantee that is "nowhere to be found" (`13562999761`). A guarantee that generates one-star reviews is worse than no guarantee.

## 9.2 Structural changes — high confidence in direction

**7. Finish the customisation work.**
*Supports:* §3.4.3 (`E01` 80, `E02` 70 — the top two themes of the 3★ and 4★ bands), §8.8 (already 80% fixed: `E01` 10.2% of E2 → 1.0% of E4), `E15` (8 reviews confirming the improvement landed).
The direction is proven and the remaining gap is specific: editing the generated programme week-by-week rather than as a whole (`12946977144`, `13072200651`), swapping running for another cardio (`E03`, 20 reviews; `E08`, 10 with medical constraints), and entering existing fitness so the plan starts at the right level (`E07`, 13).

**8. Make the escalation adaptive rather than arithmetic.**
*Supports:* `E06` (20, mean 2.85★), `E07` (13), `F08` (13), §7.4 (Germany +3.34pp on `F08`).
The complaint is consistent and partly a safety matter: targets escalate on a fixed schedule regardless of whether the user is completing them, reaching 90-minute lifts, 4-mile runs and 2 gallons of water daily for people who were sedentary at week 1 (`13329009131`, `13312781157`, `13413325683`, `12999503079`). Reviewers who complete everything are still told they have not improved (`13092628844`, `12553771048`). Adapting the ramp to observed completion would address `E06`, `E07` and `F08` together.

**9. Commission the art, or at least the hero assets.**
*Supports:* §3.4.5 (`F02` 53, 4.30%, the only rising product criticism), §7.6 (Australia 10.34%).
The objection is about craft and price together, and several reviewers state the condition for their recommendation explicitly: *"Only then can I say this is something I'd actually tell my friends about"* (`12664131139`); *"you could've at least hired an artist"* (`12366219056`); *"Just pay humans to do work"* (`13827463222`). Note the counter-evidence: `F03` (6 reviews, mean 4.50★) shows AI *planning* is welcomed. **The distinction is decoration versus function.** Fixing the typos and grammar (`G03`, 9 reviews) is the cheapest part of this and removes the most visible AI tell.

**10. Close the marketing-to-product gap on Solo Leveling.**
*Supports:* `F04` (15, mean 1.40★), `F01` (38, mean 1.68★), `A03` (38, mean **4.87★**), `H01` (15).
The positioning is the growth engine — it should not be softened. But the ad promises an RPG and the 2025 product delivered a checklist, which is what `F04` reviewers say in as many words (`12811504034`, `13053760518`, `12571594775`). Character mode (E4) is the right response and `F01` duly fell to 1.0% of E4. Finish it: the two most detailed feature requests in the corpus are both roadmaps for exactly this (`12449061267` English, `13197705864` Polish).

**11. Localise the content, not just the chrome.**
*Supports:* `G01` (16), `G02` (10), `G04` (23), §7.4 (Germany), §7.7 (Korea `G02` 18.2% of that storefront).
The app shell is translated; the book summaries and guided meditations are not. Reviewers in German, Korean, Japanese, Spanish, Portuguese and Polish storefronts all raise it (`12986937727`, `13362794086`, `13424071995`, `14269329731`, `14437535969`, `13406026400`, `14513618209`). Korea additionally reports the existing translation reads unnaturally (`13008212923`, `12939000163`, `12960946703`).

## 9.3 What not to change

- **The 66-day structure and the weekly progression.** `A08` (13, mean 4.77★) and `A01` (167, mean 4.78★) rest on it. The complaint is the *rate* of escalation (§9.2 #8), never its existence.
- **The level-up loop and the art direction.** `A02` (60, 4.55★), `A03` (38, 4.87★), `A04` (56, 3.89★). This is the moat. The `F02` objection is to the *provenance* of the assets, not to the aesthetic — several reviewers praise the look and criticise the AI in the same review (`13013464738`, `12586023608`, `13065162308`, `12992530011`).
- **Hard mode.** `A17` (13, mean 4.77★). A small, intense, entirely satisfied group (`13072368493`, `12936813410`, `13037385252`, `13218727034`).
- **The Discord community and visible developer presence.** `A09` (17, 4.65★), `A12` (19, 4.74★), both rising. Two of the corpus's most persuasive long reviews are explicitly about the developer's responsiveness (`13029589554`, `13203380781`).
- **The bundled tools.** `A11` (22, 4.00★) and `G04` (23, 4.09★). The screen blocker in particular has no detractors in this corpus.

## 9.4 Experiments worth running

| Experiment | Hypothesis | Primary metric | Grounded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| Price on screen 1 vs status quo | Disclosing up front lowers 1★ volume more than it lowers installs | 1★ rate per install; install→pay rate | §9.1 #1 |
| No-card 7-day trial vs card-gated trial | A card-free trial converts worse per-start but far better per-review | `B18`+`B01` rate; 30-day retention | §6.6 |
| Free tier of 3–5 habits | A permanent small free tier converts better than a trial | paid conversion; `B14` rate | `B14` (40); `13888253741` |
| Adaptive ramp vs fixed ramp | Escalating only on observed completion raises day-66 completion | programme completion; `E06`/`F08` rate | §9.2 #8 |
| Commissioned hero art on a subset | Removing the most visible AI tells raises willingness to pay | `F02` rate; rating | §9.2 #9 |
| Surfacing the under-18 programme | Making it visible converts the 12 self-identified minors' segment into goodwill rather than 1★ | `B11` rate among under-18 | `J02` (3), `B11` (30) |

## 9.5 Research questions this corpus raises but cannot answer

1. **What is the actual install → paywall → purchase funnel?** 331 reviews describe abandoning at the paywall, but this corpus cannot say whether that is 5% or 80% of arrivals. The single most valuable number the developer has and this report does not.
2. **Does the 4.7★ / 2.77★ gap hold in-product?** Star-only raters may be prompted in-app after a good day, while writers self-select after a bad one. Knowing where each population is captured would change how both are weighted.
3. **Did the January 2026 trial change conversion?** §6.6 shows it worsened reviews. Whether it improved revenue is unknowable here, and the two could easily point in opposite directions.
4. **How many buyers complete 66 days?** `A01` suggests a real completion cohort exists (several reviewers describe second, third and fourth cycles — `13339495032`, `13536642772`, `13216609500`) but the rate is unobservable.
5. **Is the German/Korean experience materially worse, or just differently voiced?** `A01` runs 7.69% in Germany against 18.13% in the US. Localisation quality (`G02`) is a plausible cause and is testable.
6. **Does the money-back guarantee pay for itself?** It is cited 13 times, four of them as a grievance. Its effect on conversion versus its effect on reputation is a business question this corpus only half-answers.
7. **What share of installs come from paid social?** 15 reviews name Instagram or TikTok, which is certainly an undercount. If the acquisition channel is largely paid social aimed at an anime audience, the affordability mismatch in `B11` is structural and pricing strategy has to account for it.

---

# PART 10 — EVIDENCE APPENDIX

## 10.1 Theme definitions and boundary rules

111 themes in 10 families. Each definition states what qualifies; the boundary rules exist so that a reader can re-derive any assignment. Counts and means are for the full 1,232-review corpus.

### A — Core praise (19 themes)

- **A01 · Behaviour change achieved** — Reviewer reports an actual change in their own behaviour, routine or life (habits built, back on track, life changed). Not mere intent or enthusiasm. *(n = 167, 13.56%, mean 4.78★)*
- **A02 · Gamification / level-up loved** — Praise specifically for the game layer: XP, stats, levelling, quests, character/overall rating, achievements, streak rewards. *(n = 60, 4.87%, mean 4.55★)*
- **A03 · Solo Leveling / anime fit** — Reviewer explicitly names Solo Leveling, anime, manhwa, RPG or LitRPG as the reason the app appeals. Positive direction only. *(n = 38, 3.08%, mean 4.87★)*
- **A04 · Design, art and UI praise** — Praise for visuals, art direction, interface, animations or overall polish. *(n = 56, 4.55%, mean 3.89★)*
- **A05 · Accountability and structure** — Praise for being held accountable, given direction, or having the daily decision removed. *(n = 39, 3.17%, mean 4.69★)*
- **A06 · Simplicity and ease of use** — Praise for being simple, clean, easy to navigate, low friction. *(n = 17, 1.38%, mean 4.82★)*
- **A07 · Motivation and encouragement** — Praise for motivating tone, reminders, encouragement, or being fun to return to. *(n = 40, 3.25%, mean 4.60★)*
- **A08 · Progressive difficulty praised** — Praise for the weekly ramp: tasks that start easy and get harder at a pace the reviewer finds right. *(n = 13, 1.06%, mean 4.77★)*
- **A09 · Community / Discord praised** — Positive mention of the Discord server or user community. *(n = 17, 1.38%, mean 4.65★)*
- **A10 · Best of the habit apps tried** — Explicit comparison: tried other habit trackers, this one works / is best. *(n = 15, 1.22%, mean 4.80★)*
- **A11 · In-app tools praised** — Praise for bundled tools: book summaries, guided meditation, app/screen blocker, pomodoro or time blocks, workout tracker, calorie tracker. *(n = 22, 1.79%, mean 4.00★)*
- **A12 · Developer responsiveness and updates** — Praise for frequent updates, the developer listening, or acting on feedback. *(n = 19, 1.54%, mean 4.74★)*
- **A13 · Support praised** — A support or refund interaction that went well, named as such. *(n = 9, 0.73%, mean 4.89★)*
- **A14 · Worth the money** — Explicit statement that the price is fair or the purchase was worth it. *(n = 39, 3.17%, mean 4.74★)*
- **A15 · Physical / health results** — Concrete physical outcome reported: weight lost, fitness, sleep, skin, quitting smoking or drinking. *(n = 11, 0.89%, mean 4.55★)*
- **A16 · Mental-health benefit** — Reviewer reports improved mood, confidence, depression, or purpose. *(n = 18, 1.46%, mean 4.94★)*
- **A17 · Hard mode praised** — Positive mention of Hard Mode / penalties / reset-on-failure as a feature. *(n = 13, 1.06%, mean 4.77★)*
- **A18 · ADHD or focus fit** — Reviewer self-identifies as ADHD / easily distracted and says the app suits them. *(n = 5, 0.41%, mean 3.80★)*
- **A19 · Generic praise** — Positive but unelaborated ('great app', '10/10') with no attributable reason. Never assigned alongside a specific praise theme unless a second, separate unelaborated claim is present. *(n = 109, 8.85%, mean 4.90★)*

### B — Monetization friction (19 themes)

- **B01 · Hard paywall — no free use** — The app cannot be used at all without paying. The core grievance, stated as 'not free', 'no free version', 'locked behind a paywall'. *(n = 256, 20.78%, mean 1.31★)*
- **B02 · Onboarding before price disclosure** — The questionnaire / intro / signup is completed before the price is shown; reviewer frames it as wasted time or a bait. Distinct from B01: this is about *sequence and disclosure*, not about the existence of a price. *(n = 156, 12.66%, mean 1.15★)*
- **B03 · No free trial** — Explicitly asks for, or complains about the absence of, a trial or demo. Assigned even where a trial later existed, since the review states its absence. *(n = 90, 7.31%, mean 1.32★)*
- **B04 · Price too high** — Objection to the absolute amount, independent of what is delivered. *(n = 113, 9.17%, mean 1.65★)*
- **B05 · Price versus value** — Objection that what is delivered does not justify the price. Requires a judgement about the product, not only about the number. *(n = 55, 4.46%, mean 1.93★)*
- **B06 · Plan structure mismatch** — Annual-only, missing monthly, or an annual commitment for a 66-day programme; monthly priced above annual. *(n = 15, 1.22%, mean 2.20★)*
- **B07 · Pricing misleading at checkout** — Advertised per-month figure resolves to a full-year charge, or a stated discount does not apply at purchase. *(n = 17, 1.38%, mean 1.41★)*
- **B08 · Overcharged or double-charged** — Charged more than displayed, charged twice, or billed after cancellation. *(n = 9, 0.73%, mean 1.11★)*
- **B09 · Advertised or listed as free** — The store listing or the ad said free; reviewer treats that as false. *(n = 37, 3.00%, mean 1.08★)*
- **B10 · Data collected before paywall** — Privacy objection: personal data, a signature or an account is taken before the price is disclosed. *(n = 20, 1.62%, mean 1.10★)*
- **B11 · Cannot afford — minor, student, low income** — Reviewer states they cannot pay: no job, a student, a teenager, broke. *(n = 30, 2.44%, mean 2.30★)*
- **B12 · Regional pricing and payment access** — Local-currency burden, FX-linked pricing, sanctions, or no usable payment method. *(n = 7, 0.57%, mean 2.71★)*
- **B13 · Anti-subscription — prefers one-time** — Objects to the subscription model itself; asks for a one-off purchase or paid app. *(n = 23, 1.87%, mean 1.35★)*
- **B14 · Wants a free tier, ads or donations** — Proposes a specific alternative monetisation: freemium, ad-supported, donations. *(n = 40, 3.25%, mean 1.88★)*
- **B15 · Manipulative funnel tactics** — Pledges and signatures, 'lock in' repetition, countdown or scarcity discounts, guilt framing, or a funnel the reviewer names as psychological manipulation. *(n = 35, 2.84%, mean 1.31★)*
- **B16 · Assessment result disbelieved** — The onboarding score ('40% more bad habits than your age group', a 46/100 rating) is rejected as fabricated or as a sales device. *(n = 13, 1.06%, mean 1.38★)*
- **B17 · Cannot delete account or app** — Account deletion blocked behind payment, or the app cannot be removed from the device. *(n = 10, 0.81%, mean 1.00★)*
- **B18 · Trial charged immediately** — Signed up for a stated free trial and was charged at once, or billed at trial end without warning. *(n = 11, 0.89%, mean 1.36★)*
- **B19 · Scam accusation** — Reviewer labels the app a scam, cash grab, rip-off or fraud. Recorded as sentiment, not as a finding of fact. *(n = 40, 3.25%, mean 1.00★)*

### C — Purchase evidence (6 themes)

- **C01 · Paid — confirmed** — Reviewer states they bought, subscribed, or were charged. The payer denominator. *(n = 106, 8.60%, mean 1.92★)*
- **C02 · Refund requested** — Reviewer asked for a refund or states they want their money back. *(n = 36, 2.92%, mean 1.56★)*
- **C03 · Refund denied or ignored** — A refund request was refused, unanswered, or stalled. *(n = 21, 1.70%, mean 1.14★)*
- **C04 · Cancelled or churned** — Reviewer cancelled, unsubscribed, or says they will not renew. *(n = 20, 1.62%, mean 1.95★)*
- **C05 · Money-back guarantee referenced** — Mentions the 66-day / no-questions money-back guarantee, in any direction. *(n = 13, 1.06%, mean 1.85★)*
- **C06 · Trial used** — Reviewer states they used a free trial. *(n = 9, 0.73%, mean 2.89★)*

### D — Reliability (18 themes)

- **D01 · Crash on launch / will not open** — App crashes at or before the home screen, or refuses to open. *(n = 20, 1.62%, mean 2.10★)*
- **D02 · Crash during use** — App crashes while completing a task, a reflection, or navigating. *(n = 12, 0.97%, mean 2.58★)*
- **D03 · Lag, freeze, unresponsive UI** — Slow, choppy, frozen, taps not registering, long load times. *(n = 30, 2.44%, mean 2.40★)*
- **D04 · Progress reset or lost** — Programme, day count, streak or logged data reset, wiped or rolled back. *(n = 36, 2.92%, mean 2.42★)*
- **D05 · Login and account failure** — Signed out, cannot sign back in, account no longer exists. *(n = 16, 1.30%, mean 1.50★)*
- **D06 · Signup / onboarding blocked** — Cannot get through the intro: no continue button, cannot scroll, stuck on a step. *(n = 23, 1.87%, mean 1.13★)*
- **D07 · Day advances wrongly** — App skips to the next day, opens on the wrong day, or pre-checks tasks. *(n = 20, 1.62%, mean 2.95★)*
- **D08 · Widget broken** — Home-screen widget blank, stuck on day 1, or not refreshing. *(n = 8, 0.65%, mean 3.12★)*
- **D09 · Notification bugs** — Reminders fire for completed or unassigned tasks; weekly recap miscounts. *(n = 9, 0.73%, mean 2.33★)*
- **D10 · Streak, XP, stat or rank bugs** — Streak frozen or reset without cause, XP or stat numbers wrong, achievements not credited, season rank broken. *(n = 11, 0.89%, mean 3.09★)*
- **D11 · Multi-device sync** — No sync or restore between iPhone, iPad, Mac; manual sync required. *(n = 3, 0.24%, mean 2.00★)*
- **D12 · Device or layout incompatibility** — Content cropped or off-screen, unusable on small screens, iPad or Mac layout. *(n = 15, 1.22%, mean 1.27★)*
- **D13 · An update caused a regression** — A named update broke, removed or degraded something that previously worked. *(n = 29, 2.35%, mean 3.10★)*
- **D14 · Meditation or audio broken** — Guided meditation does not play, stops, or the audio behaves wrongly. *(n = 4, 0.32%, mean 1.50★)*
- **D15 · Restore purchase fails** — Paid but the app re-prompts for payment; restore purchases does not work. *(n = 5, 0.41%, mean 1.20★)*
- **D16 · Dead buttons and links** — Support, Discord or widget buttons are inert or lead nowhere. *(n = 6, 0.49%, mean 2.17★)*
- **D17 · Character / hero mode bugs** — Hero or character mode loses names, loot, equipment, or renders blanks. *(n = 5, 0.41%, mean 3.20★)*
- **D18 · Bugs — unspecified** — Reviewer reports bugs, glitches or 'doesn't work' without naming a symptom that fits D01-D17. *(n = 48, 3.90%, mean 2.60★)*

### E — Rigidity and customisation (15 themes)

- **E01 · Cannot add own tasks** — No way to create a habit or task the app does not already offer. *(n = 80, 6.49%, mean 3.09★)*
- **E02 · Cannot edit or remove preset programme** — The generated programme's tasks and targets cannot be changed, swapped or deleted. *(n = 70, 5.68%, mean 2.54★)*
- **E03 · Running is the only cardio** — Running is mandatory or the sole cardio option; asks for walking, cycling, swimming, sport alternatives. *(n = 20, 1.62%, mean 2.75★)*
- **E04 · Schedule and day rigidity** — Fixed weekdays, no rescheduling or roll-over, frequency-per-week not supported. *(n = 37, 3.00%, mean 2.73★)*
- **E05 · Night shift / wake-time range** — Wake-up and day-start windows exclude night, graveyard or very early shifts. *(n = 31, 2.52%, mean 3.42★)*
- **E06 · Ramp unrealistic** — Weekly escalation reaches targets the reviewer considers unattainable or unsafe. *(n = 20, 1.62%, mean 2.85★)*
- **E07 · Advanced user mismatch** — Already-fit or already-disciplined reviewer is given beginner tasks, or their existing routine is not counted. *(n = 13, 1.06%, mean 3.38★)*
- **E08 · Disability, injury or health unaccommodated** — Injury, disability, asthma, nerve damage or medical limits cannot be entered. *(n = 10, 0.81%, mean 3.10★)*
- **E09 · Units — metric versus imperial** — No way to switch km/litres to miles/gallons or vice versa. *(n = 3, 0.24%, mean 3.67★)*
- **E10 · No pause for illness or absence** — No way to suspend the 66-day programme when sick, travelling or unavailable. *(n = 3, 0.24%, mean 3.67★)*
- **E11 · Day cut-off and recap timing** — The day closes or the recap fires before the reviewer's day ends (commonly 9pm). *(n = 10, 0.81%, mean 3.60★)*
- **E12 · Task library too small** — The catalogue of preset habits is too short or too narrow. *(n = 29, 2.35%, mean 3.14★)*
- **E13 · No third-party integrations** — Asks for Apple Health, Garmin, calendar, or workout-app sync. *(n = 7, 0.57%, mean 3.29★)*
- **E14 · Gender or persona fit** — Art, avatar and framing assume a young man; women or other users do not see themselves. Distinct from F09, which criticises the ideology rather than fit. *(n = 11, 0.89%, mean 2.91★)*
- **E15 · Customisation improved — noted** — Reviewer explicitly reports that editing or customisation was added or improved. *(n = 8, 0.65%, mean 4.75★)*

### F — Product-value criticism (11 themes)

- **F01 · Just a checklist** — The product reduces to a to-do list or tracker; the game and coaching layers are judged cosmetic. *(n = 38, 3.08%, mean 1.68★)*
- **F02 · AI-generated content criticised** — AI art, AI copy, AI summaries or 'AI slop' named as a defect. *(n = 53, 4.30%, mean 1.85★)*
- **F03 · AI personalisation valued** — AI-built plan or personalisation named as a positive. *(n = 6, 0.49%, mean 4.50★)*
- **F04 · Not what the ad promised** — The Solo Leveling / RPG / avatar experience advertised is absent; app differs from the marketing. *(n = 15, 1.22%, mean 1.40★)*
- **F05 · Do it yourself instead** — A notebook, Notes, Notion, ChatGPT or a free app would do the same. *(n = 36, 2.92%, mean 1.36★)*
- **F06 · Shallow or pseudo-science** — Science-backed claims judged surface-level, unevidenced, or wrong. *(n = 10, 0.81%, mean 1.80★)*
- **F07 · No enforcement or proof** — Nothing stops a user from lying; no penalty for failure; too trusting. *(n = 12, 0.97%, mean 1.50★)*
- **F08 · Tone is guilt-inducing or demotivating** — Notifications, recaps or copy that insult, shame, or declare failure; 'reset your life' framing experienced as demeaning. *(n = 13, 1.06%, mean 2.69★)*
- **F09 · Narrow audience — bro-coded** — Framing judged to target gym bros, tech bros, or a masculine self-help niche. *(n = 8, 0.65%, mean 1.62★)*
- **F10 · Onboarding too long or tedious** — The intro itself is too long or boring, independently of the paywall at its end. *(n = 29, 2.35%, mean 1.55★)*
- **F11 · Unfinished or needs polish** — General judgement that the product is early, rough or unfinished, with no specific defect named. *(n = 36, 2.92%, mean 2.64★)*

### G — Content and localisation (5 themes)

- **G01 · Localisation missing** — App, book summaries or meditations not available in the reviewer's language, or a language is requested. *(n = 16, 1.30%, mean 3.38★)*
- **G02 · Translation quality poor** — A translation exists but reads unnaturally or is inconsistent. *(n = 10, 0.81%, mean 2.60★)*
- **G03 · English typos and grammar** — Spelling and grammar errors in the English text. *(n = 9, 0.73%, mean 2.89★)*
- **G04 · Book summaries — feedback** — Any substantive comment on the book-summary library: praise, more titles, audio versions, real covers, translation. *(n = 23, 1.87%, mean 4.09★)*
- **G05 · More content requested** — Asks for more meditations, achievements, reading material, journal prompts, quotes or tasks as content. *(n = 15, 1.22%, mean 4.40★)*

### H — Feature requests (8 themes)

- **H01 · Deeper RPG — avatar, quests, penalties** — Asks for skill trees, evolving avatar, bosses, loot, random quests, or consequences for skipping. *(n = 15, 1.22%, mean 3.40★)*
- **H02 · Social — friends, guild, challenges** — Asks to see or challenge friends, form a guild, or share progress in-app. *(n = 2, 0.16%, mean 4.50★)*
- **H03 · More exercise options** — Asks for more workout types or exercise entries in the tracker. *(n = 17, 1.38%, mean 3.53★)*
- **H04 · Incremental and over-target logging** — Asks to log partial progress (cups of water) or credit for exceeding a target. *(n = 8, 0.65%, mean 3.75★)*
- **H05 · Themes and visual customisation** — Asks for dark mode, colour themes, alternative character art, custom backgrounds. *(n = 7, 0.57%, mean 3.14★)*
- **H06 · After day 66 / more programmes** — Asks what happens at the end, or for further or harder programmes. *(n = 5, 0.41%, mean 4.60★)*
- **H07 · Widget requests** — Asks for widget designs or better widget content, as a request rather than a bug. *(n = 4, 0.32%, mean 3.75★)*
- **H08 · Alarm or text-to-speech** — Asks for an in-app alarm, or audio narration of written content. *(n = 3, 0.24%, mean 3.67★)*

### I — Support (1 themes)

- **I01 · Support unreachable or unresponsive** — Support email bounces or 404s, the contact control is inert, or messages go unanswered. Excludes refund outcomes, which are C03. *(n = 21, 1.70%, mean 1.43★)*

### J — Segments and meta (9 themes)

- **J01 · Self-identified minor** — Reviewer states an age under 18. *(n = 12, 0.97%, mean 2.08★)*
- **J02 · Student / under-18 free programme** — Mentions the free access programme for students or under-18s. *(n = 3, 0.24%, mean 4.67★)*
- **J03 · Night-shift worker** — Reviewer states they work nights, graveyard or FIFO shifts. *(n = 23, 1.87%, mean 3.74★)*
- **J04 · Referral code in review** — Review text contains a referral code. *(n = 1, 0.08%, mean 4.00★)*
- **J05 · Review prompted before use** — Reviewer states they were asked to rate before using the product. *(n = 4, 0.32%, mean 2.25★)*
- **J06 · No substantive content** — Text carries no assessable information ('Good', 'H', an emoji, 'Updated'). *(n = 28, 2.27%, mean 4.39★)*
- **J07 · Alleges fake reviews** — Claims the positive reviews are bots, paid or AI-generated. *(n = 8, 0.65%, mean 1.00★)*
- **J08 · Discovered via social ad** — Names Instagram, TikTok or a social ad as the acquisition source. *(n = 15, 1.22%, mean 2.47★)*
- **J09 · Rating contradicts text** — Star rating and text point in opposite directions (5-star complaint, 1-star praise). Disclosure theme; never used as a sentiment measure. *(n = 27, 2.19%, mean 3.67★)*

## 10.5 Counting rules, denominators and reconciliation summary

**Counting rules.**
1. A review counts once toward a theme regardless of how many times it raises it. There are no duplicate theme assignments inside a review (validated).
2. Themes are non-exclusive. The 1,232 reviews carry **3,073 theme assignments**, a mean of **2.49 per review**. Theme percentages therefore sum to far more than 100%.
3. Family counts are **unions**, not sums: a review carrying three `B` themes contributes 1 to the `B` family count.
4. **Sentiment is not inferred from rating.** A theme's direction comes from its definition; its mean★ is reported alongside so that direction and rating can be compared rather than conflated.
5. `A19` (generic praise) is assigned only where no specific praise theme applies, so it never double-counts an attributable compliment.
6. `D18` (unspecified bugs) is assigned only where no symptom matching `D01`–`D17` is described.
7. Every percentage in the report states its denominator explicitly. Where a denominator other than 1,232 is used, it is named in the sentence or the table header.

**Denominators used in this report.**

| Denominator | Value | Where used |
|---|---:|---|
| Full corpus | 1,232 | All global findings, all signal classification |
| Product-experience subset | 901 | §4.1 only, clearly marked |
| Payer segment | 106 | §6, always labelled "segment rate" |
| Rating bands | 507 / 119 / 111 / 136 / 359 | §5.2–5.6 |
| Eras | 59 / 384 / 499 / 290 | §8 |
| Trial boundary | 986 / 246 | §6.6, §8.6 |
| Eligible storefronts | 579 / 102 / 91 / 79 / 58 | §7.2–7.6 |
| High-spend group | 980 | §7.8 |
| High-volume group | 1,028 | §7.9 |

**Reconciliation summary.** 1,232 records in `reviews.jsonl`; 1,232 unique review IDs; 1,232 records across 75 `by_country` files with zero IDs in one file and not the other; `manifest.json` matches on total, storefront count, rating distribution, mean and all 75 per-country counts; `_state.json` shows all 140 storefronts complete with `collected == total` everywhere and per-country figures matching. **1,232 reviews read individually (100%). 1,232 classified (100%).** Zero unassigned, zero unknown IDs, zero empty theme lists. Full detail in §1.3.

## 10.6 Provenance of this analysis

The classification behind this report was made by reading every review individually, in its original language, in date order, in 21 chunks of 60. Judgements were recorded against a stable working index (1..1232) and resolved deterministically to `review_id` by script, so that no ID was ever transcribed by hand. The taxonomy was built from the corpus rather than imported.

Eleven integrity assertions run before the report is written (§1.3) and three verification passes run after (§1.5 step 9). The report generator refuses to emit a file if any placeholder is unfilled or any of 22 recomputed count checks disagrees with the text.

**Known error risk.** The borderline calls are concentrated in `B01`/`B02`, `E01`/`E02` and `D18`/specific-symptom, each with a stated rule in §10.1. Every theme with fewer than 10 reviews was re-read end to end before publication, as were all 106 payer reviews, all 40 scam accusations and all 27 star/text contradictions. No automated classifier was used at any point, so there is no model error to disclose — the error mode here is human judgement on ambiguous text, and the per-theme ID lists in §10.8 exist so that any reader can check it.
